| D53 | **Control/safety operability shipped — DROP-from-faulted, acknowledge_fault latency, restart auto-finalize** (22 Jun 2026, #210/#332/#331) | **Three operability fixes, all safety-reviewed: (1) #210 — DROP BEANS is now allowed from a FAULTED run (dump beans off a hot drum); only DROP is added to the faulted action matrix, heat stays OFF. (2) #332 — acknowledge_fault no longer blocks behind the latched-escalation MCP re-read once acknowledged (the #320 fault-latch was starving the action queue). (3) #331 — a stale prior-session FAULTED run is auto-finalized on restart rather than restored as ACTIVE (store-write-only, NO heat/fan resume — restart still enters operator_recovery_required for a genuinely active run).** | **Roast-2/3 evidence: a faulted hot drum needs an operator escape (drop the beans), the fault-latch made acknowledge sluggish, and a faulted run surviving a restart stranded the boot UI. None of these weaken the invariants: heat stays off in fault, #331 never resumes (it finalizes a dead run, not an active one), and restart-with-a-possibly-active-run still requires explicit operator action.** Cross-refs: #210/#332/#331, #320 (the fault-latch), the restart→operator_recovery_required invariant. |
| D54 | **Two operator-facing fault-semantics decisions** (22 Jun 2026, both safety-reviewed PASS, #348 / #350+#329) | **(a) #348 — acknowledging a fault while the bean is STILL above the hard ceiling FINALISES that tick rather than auto-escalating to emergency stop. Heat is already off in fault; e-stop stays reachable from every phase. (b) #350/#329 — a hot fault that SURVIVES a restart no longer auto-offers in-app cooling for that (now-finalized) run; the future "cool from idle / history" button is the planned enhancement.** | **(a) Once heat is off and the operator has consciously acknowledged, auto-e-stopping on temperature alone is redundant and removes operator agency; the e-stop path remains available if they want it. (b) Consistent with #331 (a restart-surviving fault is finalized, not active) — you cannot drive cooling on a run the system has finalized; surfacing a deliberate idle/history cooling action is the right shape, deferred. Both preserve fail-closed (heat off) and keep e-stop universally reachable.** Cross-refs: #348, #350/#329, D53/#331, the e-stop-from-every-phase invariant. |
| D55 | **Chart/UX + observability shipped, smoothing chosen, test-gate hardened; session follow-ups recorded** (22 Jun 2026) | **Shipped:** #326 (chart x-axis + cursor = charge-referenced ROAST TIME, preheat negative), #307 (temp axis auto-range + hysteresis, no clip/collapse; RoR fixed; heat/fan a subordinate overlay; charge band in frame), #309 (chart event markers — charge/T0, FC, drop, cooling, all server-sourced), #325 (advisory-history rows show roast-time + bean-temp), #324 (home/menu landing hub + persistent nav + state-aware `/`), #330 (preheat clock freezes on a terminal/fault phase), #315 (bean-profile product/source URL + hardened validation), #329 (restored/reloaded fault renders ACKNOWLEDGE from the hydrated snapshot). **Chart smoothing decided: centred Savitzky–Golay** on bean + RoR (operator-approved, #344, build pending). **Test/infra:** #338 (via #340) lossless e2e settle barrier + idempotent replay stepping — fixed the flaky merge gate that was blocking the pipeline; follow-up #339 SSE Last-Event-ID resume (the live SPA silently drops events on reconnect today). | **The live chart was the operator's main instrument in roast 3 (D48); this session closed the readability/observability gaps and the SPA-robustness/test-gate gaps that the session surfaced. SPA still renders from server data only.** **Open follow-ups filed this session (beyond the cleared backlog):** #344 (SG smoothing — build), #346 (server-side `_roast_elapsed_seconds` freeze on terminal phases — makes the #330 client hold redundant), #351 (server DRYING_END signal → then the chart dry-end marker), #337 (the agent must HONOR MCP's backdated T0/FC timestamp — gated on coffee-roaster-mcp #169/#170 / v0.1.7; auggie-verified the agent uses `self._clock()` today, not the MCP timestamp). Cross-refs: D48 (chart direction), D49/#337 (backdating, now the agent-side consumer), #169/#170 (the gating MCP release). |
| D56 | **#346 closed as superseded by the #330 client-side readout freeze — the run-elapsed value must stay LIVE server-side** (23 Jun 2026; corrects the D55 framing that #346 "makes the #330 hold redundant") | **#346 proposed freezing `RoastController._roast_elapsed_seconds` server-side on terminal phases. Implementation revealed the premise is wrong: `roast_elapsed_seconds` / per-row `elapsed_seconds` is a SINGLE multi-purpose field driving THREE consumers — (1) the curve x-axis (`dashboardReducer` keys each plotted point on serve elapsed, #326); (2) the persistence throttle (`store.record_telemetry` only inserts a row when elapsed has advanced ≥ the interval); (3) the operator readout. A blanket server freeze of the shared field collapses every cooling point onto one x AND stops cooling rows persisting (the suite caught a completed-cooling replay dropping 18→14 points, and it breaks the #338 settle barrier). Only consumer (3) should freeze, and #330 already freezes it CLIENT-SIDE keyed on the server phase. So the live server value is CORRECT for (1)+(2); the readout-freeze is a pure presentation concern and belongs at the presentation layer, not the controller. **#346 closed won't-fix / superseded; no code change.** | **The #346 issue mis-located the bug as server-side. The advancing server clock is by-design for the curve + throttle; conflating "the run clock the operator reads" with "the per-row elapsed the curve/throttle depend on" (they share one field today) is what made a server freeze look right. The SPA-renders-from-server-data invariant is preserved — #330 reads the server PHASE and holds the display, it does not infer phase. If a frozen final-elapsed is ever wanted in the PERSISTED snapshot/timeline (e.g. a post-roast report), add a SEPARATE display field — never freeze the load-bearing shared one. Also this session: #339 (SSE Last-Event-ID resume) and #344 (centred Savitzky–Golay smoothing) — both D55 follow-ups — SHIPPED + merged. Cross-refs: D55 (filed #346, framing corrected here), #330 (the client hold — the right layer), #326 (curve x-axis keying), #338 (settle barrier), the SPA-renders-from-server invariant. Evidence: implementation finding, 23 Jun.** |
| D57 | **The agent honours MCP v0.1.7's backdated T0/FC timestamps — and that is a CONTROL coupling, not display-only** (23 Jun 2026, #337 via #362; coffee-roaster-mcp v0.1.7 released) | **MCP v0.1.7 (#169/#170) backdates the reported T0 (turning point) and FC (crack onset) event timestamps. The agent now honours them by applying the backdating DELTA (`confirmed_at_monotonic − onset`, computed entirely in the MCP child process's own monotonic domain — it NEVER assigns the absolute cross-process `monotonic_seconds`, which is non-comparable, D6) to its own receive-tick clock, so `_charge_monotonic` / `_first_crack_monotonic` anchor ~15-20 s earlier. Fails closed to receive-tick when the new payload fields are absent/invalid (a manual mark, a pre-0.1.7 payload, or a negative/non-finite delta) — the future-rejection contract holds (a backdated stamp is ≤ now). Pin bumped `coffee-roaster-mcp` 0.1.6 → 0.1.7. | **Independent safety review (D23) corrected the author's "display-only / bounds nothing" self-assessment: the backdated clocks feed `Controller._development_percent`, which the #313 drop-coherence guard reads to REJECT vs allow an advisor drop. So honouring the backdate raises system dev% ~+1.8 pp and releases the advisor drop ~1-2 pp EARLIER — on a TRUER dev% (the receive-tick FC was ~17 s late, understating development). It fails safe: a guard REJECT is still a held drop, no roaster write; no transition/enum/verdict/Fahrenheit change; e-stop + restart-no-auto-resume intact; the operator-mark-FC override can't pick up the backdate stage. The #327 trim's FC-ETA is charge-clock-INDEPENDENT (verified — a constant offset cancels in the RoR slope difference), so the trim is unaffected. **Operator decision (confirmed, not relayed): ACCEPT the ~1-2 pp-earlier release with `drop_dev_margin_percent` UNCHANGED at the 3 pp default (it was calibrated against the OLD lag-biased dev%); RE-CHECK where the advisor drop actually releases on ROAST 4 and tune the margin only if needed.** A rare advisory drift (a `_persist_t0_if_charged` retry succeeding after drop persists a slightly-late charge UTC) was triaged DOCUMENTED — advisory-only breadcrumb, pragma-rare path, no safety gate reads it. Reviews: safety not-blocking, mcp-contract-checker PASS (consumed field names match 0.1.7 exactly → no silent no-op), claude-review no-blockers. Cross-refs: coffee-roaster-mcp #169/#170 + v0.1.7, D49 (the backdating decision, consumer side), #313 (drop-coherence guard), #327 (trim — unaffected), memory `roast4-drop-release-watch-337`. |
| D58 | **A skip-aware `review-gate` required status check closes the auto-merge-before-`claude-review` race** (24 Jun 2026, #159 via #366; operator-approved "build gate + operator activates") | **`.github/workflows/review-gate.yml` publishes a commit STATUS context `review-gate` on each PR head SHA: PR opened/synchronized → `pending`; the `Claude Code Review` workflow completing (a `workflow_run` trigger keyed to that workflow name) → `success` **only when its conclusion is `success`** (fail-closed: a failed/cancelled review leaves the `pending` in place, so a crashed review can't merge un-reviewed); a Dependabot PR or any PR editing `.github/workflows/**` → `success` **immediately at stamp time** (those are exactly the classes where `Claude Code Review` legitimately cannot run — no secret / workflow-validation 401 — and the reason it is un-required). Once `review-gate` is marked a REQUIRED check on `main`, repo auto-merge then WAITS for it, so a PR can no longer merge in the ~1-2 min before the ~10-min review posts its inline findings. **Marking it required is a deliberate operator branch-protection step (main has `enforce_admins` on); the PR ships only the mechanism.** Activation sequencing is load-bearing: MERGE the workflow to the default branch FIRST (so `workflow_run` fires), THEN require the check — never the reverse, or normal PRs hang on a `pending` that never resolves. | **Rationale:** `required_conversation_resolution` only blocks on threads that ALREADY exist (D37 keeps `claude-review` NON-required because it fails-by-design on workflow/Dependabot PRs and passes-on-findings), so if the required CI checks go green before the slow review posts, auto-merge fires and the findings gate is bypassed by timing (#157 was the benign near-miss). review-gate asserts the review *ran*, not that it was clean — findings stay gated by the inline threads + conversation-resolution, so it composes with D37 without re-introducing the workflow/Dependabot deadlock that un-required `claude-review` in the first place (the exempt classes pass at STAMP time, not via the flip). The fail-closed flip (success-only) was an Augment finding folded pre-merge. **VALIDATED end-to-end on three real PRs the same session** (#366 auto-passed as its own workflow edit; #367/#368 stamped `pending` → flipped `success` after the review posted). Operator interim rule "don't `--auto` substantive PRs" (D-less, #159) is now superseded by the mechanism once activated. Cross-refs: D37 (roster + why `claude-review` is non-required), D23 (independent review/triage), AGENTS.md "PR Merge Policy" / `claude-review-not-a-required-check` / `ci-green-is-not-mergeable` memories, #159/#366, `.github/workflows/{review-gate,claude-code-review}.yml`. Recorded as part of the 24 Jun pre-roast-4 reliability batch (#212 bounded wedged-child shutdown, #177 persist-stop-unconfirmed + heat-off retry, #306 → coffee-roaster-mcp v0.1.8 console-flood quiet) — the measured "after-v2" PR-hygiene sample (memory `pr-flow-improvement-experiment`). |
| D59 | **Pre-FC heat/fan are profile-parameterised deterministic levers, not live operator dials — option (C)** (24 Jun 2026, #318; refines D35) | **Pre-FC the controller deterministically re-asserts heat/fan every tick (D35); today those targets come from the GLOBAL `PreFirstCrackLevers` config (heat 100 / fan 30), IGNORING the bean profile — so a per-bean pre-FC value is unreachable via the UI (overwritten within a tick — observed roast 2) AND via the profile (ignored). Decision: `RoastControlPolicy` sources the pre-FC heat/fan targets from the ACTIVE bean profile via NEW explicit `pre_fc_heat` / `pre_fc_fan` fields, falling back to the `PreFirstCrackLevers` config default when the profile does not specify. The live dashboard renders pre-FC heat/fan as READ-OUTS (not dials). The profile value MUST validate inside the pre-FC safety box (keep `fan_ceiling`).** | **Rejected (A) read-out-only (merely hides the control — a delicate natural still cannot lower pre-FC fan) and (B) live free-hand override (re-opens the free-hand pre-FC control D35/#218 deliberately closed). (C) keeps the controller OWNING the loop deterministically (D35 intact, no live override, honours the minimal-UX "heat/fan are read-outs not dials" decision) while moving the deterministic VALUE to where craft belongs — the per-bean profile, now that the bean-profile library (#303) exists. Controller-owns-the-loop + every-write-through-safety invariants preserved (the profile value is bounded by the pre-FC box). Non-blocking for roast 4 (default fan 30 is proven). Operator decision, 24 Jun.** Cross-refs: D35 (the advisor split), #218 (controller owns pre-FC), #303 (bean-profile library), the 13-Jun minimal-UX decision (`automated-roaster-minimal-ux`), `operator-working-n8n-roast-system` (pre-FC deterministic). |

| D61 | **Graceful shutdown is heat-off only — auto-cooling declined** (24 Jun 2026, #176 CLOSED; heat-off shipped #142) | **Graceful shutdown cuts heat to 0 (the MUST, shipped in #142) and does NOT auto-engage cooling / fan. #176 closed as declined.** | **The Hottop cooling path on `drop_beans` / cooling is an UNVERIFIED item (component plan §3); commanding an unverified cooling path on shutdown is an unsafe-when-uncertain write, whereas heat-off is unambiguously safe (fail-closed). For roast 1 the operator guides light to avoid a dark roast, knowing audio FC lags ~30 s so true development runs longer than the number. **Priority = watch the AUTO-drop fire** (should land light ~188–193 before the 195 ceiling); the **un-gated operator manual drop is the backstop** (`controller.py` — the #313 coherence guard gates ONLY the advisor drop, never the operator DROP BEANS). | D63 | **FC-lag offset belongs in coffee-roaster-mcp as optional config, not the agent — the MCP reports the corrected FC, agent + UI follow** (27 Jun 2026, operator; DIRECTION agreed, build + ratify POST-roast-4; revises D49/D57) | Audio FC lags the true crack (~30 s observed; #337/D57 already onset-backdates in the MCP). The residual empirical lag should be a NEW optional MCP config — `first_crack.onset_offset_seconds` (default 0; tunable in the YAML, no code release per tune) — applied ON TOP of the onset backdate, so the MCP reports a single corrected FC event. The agent computes dev% from it (no agent-side offset → no double-correction); the UI follows. **Filed: agent #380 (consumer + tracking); a `coffee-roaster-mcp` companion to follow.** | **Single source of truth for FC timing (the MCP owns the detector + already backdates), keeps the recorded corpus consistent (MCP exports carry the corrected FC), avoids the agent stacking a correction on the MCP backdate, and satisfies the D49 "don't have the AGENT invent a correction" caution. A corrected (higher) dev% releases the auto-drop EARLIER ⇒ mitigates the #323 guard-vs-ceiling overshoot. Safety-adjacent (moves where the #313 drop guard's dev% originates) → safety-reviewer + mcp-contract-checker on the pin bump; tune the offset to roast-4 captured data first.** Cross-refs: D49 (onset backdating), D57/#337 (the MCP onset backdate this extends), #323 (overshoot), #313 (drop guard), #380, `fc-detector-lag`, memory `roast4-in-progress`. |
| D65 | **Recording = two INDEPENDENT USB-mic streams (option A, not sample-locked); recording is best-effort and DECOUPLED from the FC read loop AND its lifecycle** (27 Jun 2026, operator + a deep-research pass; revises D64's hardware choice; from the roast-5 recording regression) | **(a) Hardware = option A: TWO independent USB-mic streams, NOT sample-locked.** A single 2-channel USB interface (D64) is the only TRUE sample-lock, but the 27 Jun deep-research confirmed sample-lock is UNNECESSARY for FC training — independent streams are fine — so the simpler two-mic option A wins (and works on both macOS + Pi). Capture is config-driven (`recording` section: both mics, sample_rate, autocapture, export_location); the MCP gained `set_recording_metadata(origin, roast_num)` (the 14th tool) + `record_mics`-compatible naming `mic{N}-{origin}-roast{N}.wav` + a session JSON. **(b) The HARD invariant surfaced by roast 5: recording is BEST-EFFORT and must NEVER perturb FC detection — decoupled from BOTH the detector read loop AND its lifecycle.** Never do blocking work on the detector's capture-read worker: roast 5 aborted because the per-block WAV flush packed each 16 k-sample block one-by-one via a `struct.pack` Python loop (GIL ~3.6 ms) inside the detector's read worker → stalled the read → 30 consecutive mic overflows → audio faulted, FC dead, operator stopped pre-FC (coffee-roaster-mcp #180; FIXED — numpy-vectorised flush, 0.28 ms / 13× / byte-identical PCM16; soak-validated). And the recorder must OUTLIVE FC detection to capture the FULL roast — today it is owned by the FC-detection pipeline which stops at FC, so capture truncates at the crack (coffee-roaster-mcp #181). **(c) Release state (UPDATED 28 Jun, D67): MCP 0.1.9 shipped #175 (per-window FC confidence) + #176 (config-driven multi-device capture + the 14th tool); MCP 0.1.10 shipped #180 (flush) + #162 (per-request log flood); MCP 0.1.11 shipped #181 (recorder lifecycle → full charge→drop recording, D67) + #178 (live mic peak/RMS levels). Agent pinned 0.1.11.** **(d) The per-roast OBJECTIVE label = weight-loss % (#388: capture roasted weight, compute % loss) alongside the operator rating — both feed D42.** | **Roast 6 proved the full stack end-to-end (deterministic trim engaged, FIRST auto-FC detected on a real roast, advisor drove a clean dev%-gated drop under the ceiling, both mics captured), but only AFTER the roast-5 abort taught the decoupling invariant the hard way: a best-effort observability feature (recording) reached into the safety-critical detection path and killed FC. Option A removes the macOS-aggregate-vs-Pi-ALSA + single-interface hardware constraint of D64 at zero cost to FC training (research-confirmed). Weight-loss % is a cheap, objective corpus label the operator rating can't replace (taste is subjective; mass loss is physics). The overflow bites HARDER on the Pi 5 (RP1 USB, onnx_threads=2, int8) so #180 is necessary but maybe not sufficient there — tracked as E11-S3 (Pi dual-mic + FC CPU soak).** Cross-refs: D64 (the capture decision this revises), D27 (Pi inference budget / E11-S3), D42 (learning loop + labels), coffee-roaster-mcp #175/#176 (0.1.9) + #180/#181/#162 (0.1.10) + #178 (mic-levels check) + #179 (pin pyright), agent #385 (store-backed roast_num) / #386 (adaptive trim depth) / #387 (T0 still ~11 s late) / #388 (weight-loss label), memory `roast4-in-progress`, `fc-audio-overflow-fatal`. |
| D64 | **FC training-data capture is MCP-owned + config-driven; a single 2-channel USB interface; capture-only v1 (no in-system annotation)** (27 Jun 2026, operator; from the roast-4 FC-detector miss) | **The audio FC detector MISSED on roast 4 (operator marked FC manually; zero confidence observability — coffee-roaster-mcp #175). Decision: build a roast→training-data flywheel. v1 = the MCP CAPTURES roast audio (it already owns the device for detection — read once, tee to inference + WAV), config-driven (`recording.autocapture` / `export_location` / `device` / `channels` / `sample_rate`; optional start/stop commands). Output: one WAV per channel + a session JSON carrying the model-FC + per-window confidence timeline in RECORDING-relative seconds (the MCP owns both clocks → sample-accurate, no sync tone). Hardware: a single class-compliant **2-channel USB interface** (one sample-locked device on BOTH macOS + Pi) — CONDITIONAL on Pi-5 CPU headroom alongside the torch-free inference (D27). NO in-system annotation — Label Studio labels FC start/end post-roast; the RoastPilot FC button stays a pure human OVERRIDE (no FC-end button, minimal UI). OUT OF SCOPE (later): auto-annotation, the operator-FC-vs-MCP-FC validation-over-time view, fine-tune/retrain.** | **Roast 4 proved the detector can silently miss (a washed Colombian crack / 40 °C ambient / mic) with no confidence observability to diagnose it. Capturing every roast (2 mics) + the MCP confidence timeline turns each roast into a labelled training example AND a model-vs-operator datapoint (feeds D42). MCP-owned capture is what makes the recording↔detection clock mapping exact (Fork 3). The single 2-channel interface kills the macOS-aggregate-vs-Pi-ALSA split + the 2-USB-mic drift that breaks `propagate_annotations`' sample-lock assumption. Capture-only v1 keeps autonomy/UX minimal and defers annotation/validation complexity. Reuses the existing `coffee-first-crack-detection` pipeline (`record_mics.py`, Label Studio, `convert_labelstudio_export`, `propagate_annotations`, `chunk_audio`).** Cross-refs: coffee-roaster-mcp #175 (confidence observability — do first) + #176 (the v1 capture build), #174 (T0 onset), `coffee-first-crack-detection` (pipeline + corpus), D42 (learning loop), D27 (Pi inference budget), memory `roast4-in-progress`. |
| D66 | **T0 is correctly stamped at the turning point; the auto-T0 backdate is authoritative; the "~11 s late" reading was a measurement artifact — #387 closed as NOT a bug** (28 Jun 2026, #387; re-diagnosed from the roast-6 store trace; the #174 T0 fix stands) | **The roast-6 store trace shows the auto-T0 origin (`beans_added`) lands at the turning point within ~2 s (sampling granularity), NOT ~11 s late. The earlier "~11 s late" was a CHART-READ ARTIFACT: a 170 °C *rising* bean-temp crossing was misread as the turning point, when the true turning point is the 174 °C running-max *peak* ~14 s later. Auggie-confirmed against `coffee-roaster-mcp` `session.py:1590-1630`: the MCP backdates `beans_added` to the running-max bean-temp peak, and `auto_t0_drop_threshold_c` controls ONLY detection latency / the backdate *delta* — it NEVER moves the stamped origin. Resolution (no behaviour change): a turning-point regression test + lowering the *example* `auto_t0_drop_threshold_c` 25→15 (robustness only). #387 closed as not-a-bug; the T0-accuracy question is CLOSED.** | **An apparent T0 inaccuracy was carried as an open suspect across two session notes ("backdate not reaching the turning point; suspects: threshold too coarse / MCP backdate short / agent delta"). Grounding it against the actual store trace + the MCP source dissolved all three suspects: the origin is the bean-temp peak by construction, and the threshold is a detection-latency lever, not an origin lever. Recording this prevents re-opening a non-bug and re-tuning a threshold that cannot move the stamped T0 — the right artefact to distrust was the chart read, not the code. The #174 auto-T0 fix is confirmed correct on hardware.** Cross-refs: #387, #174 (the auto-T0 fix), `coffee-roaster-mcp` `session.py` (backdate-to-peak), D49/D57/D63 (FC/T0 timing), memory `roast4-in-progress`, `store-telemetry-event-clock-mismatch` (read the right store row, not the chart). |
| D67 | **#181 implements D65's recorder-lifecycle decouple — recording spans the FULL roast (charge→drop), not charge→FC; shipped in MCP 0.1.11** (28 Jun 2026, #181; auggie-confirmed; satisfies D65(b)) | **The recorder finalises at session **STOP**, not at first crack — recordings now span charge→drop. Previously the recorder was owned by the FC-detection pipeline, which stops at FC, so capture truncated at the crack. Auggie-confirmed the FC training pipeline `chunk_audio.py` slides a window across the FULL recording, so truncating at FC was discarding ~80 % of the **negative-class** (pre-/post-crack non-FC) training data — the class the detector most needs to learn the crack boundary against. Released in **coffee-roaster-mcp 0.1.11** (bundled with #178 live mic peak/RMS dBFS levels on FC-status); agent pinned 0.1.11. | D82 | **#405 post-FC control redesign — the three design calls are RESOLVED (operator, 1 Jul 2026). (1) Post-FC heat becomes a DETERMINISTIC RoR-target closed loop (NOT the advisor); (2) DROP becomes a deterministic eligibility rule (`bean_temp ≥ style.drop_temp_c` AND `dtr ≥ style.dtr_target`) with the LLM able to pull the drop EARLIER only, never later; (3) a new `RoastStyle` roast-style dimension parameterises the drop targets. That is how gpt-4o+c3 (roast 7) crashed heat to 0 + ramped fan 50→100 at FC — coherent rationales but a *formula* (cut heat, ramp fan, drop at DTR≈target) executed without the judgment to hold a heat floor to the drop TEMP → 188 °C / 15.86 % under-temp drop; gpt-4.1-mini+c6 (roast 8) still crashed 40→0 one tick post-FC but RECOVERED ~30 s later (solid 193 °C / 21.1 %) — the prompt layer mitigates, it does not prevent. **Redesign = three thin slices, each touching `controller.py`/`safety.py`/`models.py` enums routed through `safety-reviewer` (Opus) pre-open; validated on the bake-off harness AND a supervised hardware roast before "done".** **Slice A (first, additive, independent of the heat law):** `RoastStyle` — a plain `Enum` (D15, not `StrEnum`) {LIGHT, MEDIUM, DARK} → (`drop_temp_c`, `dtr_target`), seeded from the per-origin corpus for washed high-grown: **LIGHT 188 °C / 15 %, MEDIUM 193 °C / 18 % (default), DARK 196 °C / 20 %**; the 195–196 °C bitter cap holds regardless; per-profile override retained (today's single `target_drop_temp_c`/`target_development_percent` become style-parameterised, back-compat preserved). **Slice B (safety-critical core):** replace crash-to-0 with a deterministic **RoR-target closed loop** — hold a post-FC RoR band by adjusting heat, bounded every write by the safety box; roast 7/8's 188/15.86 under-temp drop is the negative case it must fix. **Slice C:** deterministic drop-eligibility anchor (temp ≥ style AND DTR ≥ style) fires the drop; the advisor may only recommend an EARLIER drop (never delay) — the trace showed the LLM added ~no value at the drop (a DTR≥target rule), so this removes the formula-follow while keeping the advisor advisory-only. Invariants held: every post-FC write still passes safety policy (CLAMP/REJECT); emergency stop from every phase; restart→`operator_recovery_required` (no auto-resume); Celsius throughout; the advisor never gets MCP writes (this is a *controller* law). | **The operator OVERRODE the lead's "fixed heat floor first, RoR-target as a later refinement" recommendation and chose the RoR-target closed loop NOW (call 1). The trade recorded honestly: the RoR loop is more capable (it holds a controlled decline rather than a static floor, directly targeting the roast-7 stall-vs-overshoot band) but it moves gain tuning + anti-windup + RoR-noise handling into the safety-critical control path, so it RAISES the validation bar — it cannot be signed off on replay alone; a supervised hardware roast is a hard gate (D35a's "a replay validates the decision, not the heat-management" caveat applies doubly here). Calls 2 (deterministic drop + LLM-earlier-only) and 3 (default MEDIUM, corpus-seeded values) matched the lead recommendation. Why deterministic-drop-with-LLM-earlier over keep-LLM: roasts 7/8 proved the LLM's post-FC value was a threshold rule the controller can own; reserving the LLM for an *earlier* pull keeps the one place operator judgment could still help (calling a fast roast early) without letting a formula delay the drop past the style temp. Why a style dimension: roast 7's 188/16 % is a valid LIGHT roast, not a failure — making style an explicit dial turns an accidental outcome into an operator choice and gives Slice B/C a per-style target to aim at. Roast priority UNCHANGED — #405 IS the top control priority; this decision starts it. Slice A opens first (additive enum, lowest risk); B and C follow, each safety-reviewed pre-open.** Cross-refs: #405 (the issue + the 1 Jul scope comment this ratifies), roast 7 / roast 8 (the over-brake evidence), D35 (the control split at FC this refines — post-FC was LLM-advises-levers; D82 makes post-FC heat deterministic + reserves the LLM for an earlier-drop pull), D43/D69 (the gpt-4o+c1 heat-fidelity pin — the post-FC *heat* problem D82's Slice B fixes deterministically), D15 (plain-Enum typed vocabulary — `RoastStyle`), D7 (inline per-roast profiles — style rides the profile), memories `roast7-findings`, `roast8-findings`, `per-origin-dtr-washed-highgrown`, `operator-working-n8n-roast-system`. |

When active the CONTROLLER owns both post-FC levers — heat via the PI loop, fan pinned to a deterministic config value — and the advisor's `target_heat`/`target_fan` are NO LONGER actuated post-FC (the advisor becomes drop-only; `should_drop` still honored, tightened in Slice C). Shape: (5) ships behind `post_fc_ror_loop_enabled` (default `False` = byte-for-byte today's advisor-driven post-FC), flipped ON only post-hardware-roast — the exact #412/#386 de-risk path; (6) when ON the controller owns BOTH post-FC levers (heat = PI loop, fan = a deterministic `post_fc_fan` config value) and the advisor's lever outputs are IGNORED post-FC — this kills BOTH halves of the roast-7 over-brake (heat→0 AND fan 50→100), not just heat; the advisor still returns `should_drop` (honored via the existing #313 coherence guard until Slice C makes the drop a deterministic eligibility rule + LLM-earlier-only). **Architecture:** today DEVELOPMENT has no deterministic target (`PhaseControlLimits.has_deterministic_target` is pre-FC only), so Slice B extends the deterministic control path into DEVELOPMENT: the PI loop computes a heat target each control tick, and — per the #412 told==enforced control-path catch — the DEVELOPMENT safety box's `heat_floor_percent`/`heat_target_percent` are built FROM the actuated PI output (never an undamped target), the write goes through `evaluate_command` like every other, and the PI/integrator state advances ONLY after an ACCEPTED write (rate-limited/REJECT ticks consume no integrator budget). A minimum heat floor (> 0) makes crash-to-0 structurally impossible. Invariants held: every post-FC write passes safety policy (CLAMP/REJECT); the 196 °C bitter ceiling + emergency-drop bound are unchanged and still clamp the loop; emergency stop from every phase; restart→`operator_recovery_required` (the loop never auto-resumes); Celsius throughout; the advisor never receives MCP writes (this is a controller law). | **D82 set the direction (RoR loop over fixed floor); D83 records the concrete, safety-reviewable spec so the build is not improvised in a PR. Why controller-owns-both-levers over loop-owns-heat-only: leaving the advisor's fan-brake live would let it oppose the heat loop (the roast-7 over-brake was heat AND fan), re-introducing the thrash the loop exists to remove; making the advisor drop-only post-FC is also the clean seam Slice C formalises. | D87 | **#405 post-FC control REVISED after the first hardware A/B (9 Jul): the fixed-band RoR-target loop (D83) is WRONG post-FC — it adds heat to hold the band, causing overroast. Post-FC control must be a controlled DECLINING-RoR taper + a hard ceiling-guard drop; #460 (enable-flip) STAYS OFF until reworked. Supersedes the D83 fixed-band design.** (9 Jul 2026, hardware A/B — operator + agent) | **The supervised A/B (roast 1 baseline advisor-driven vs roast 2 #405 deterministic) on Guatemala El Durazno ran exactly as the enable-gate intended and caught a real design flaw. Baseline was clean (drop 189 °C, DTR 13.6 %, advisor ramped fan + called the drop). But with #405 ON, post-FC RoR naturally declined to ~5 °C/min while the loop's fixed target is ~8 (D83 `target_ror_c_per_min`); the loop saw 5 < 8 and drove HEAT to 89 % to chase the band — the opposite of what post-FC wants (a gentle declining RoR to the drop). Bean raced toward the 196 bitter ceiling; the advisor CORRECTLY wanted heat 0 (brake, extend development) but under #405 the advisor's heat is advisory-only and was overridden. Operator caught it + manually dropped ~193. The mirror-image failure is also real (operator observation): a hard advisor cut straight to 0 % over-brakes, collapsing the RoR (roast-7/8 class) — so BOTH a fixed-band loop and a hard-0 advisor fail in OPPOSITE directions. Two concrete bugs: (1) the RoR loop adds heat to hold a fixed band — post-FC it must target a DECAYING setpoint, or only trim/brake, never add heat; (2) the deterministic drop (bean ≥ target AND dev ≥ target) can sail past the bitter ceiling while waiting for dev % — needs a hard ceiling-guard (drop on bean ≥ ceiling regardless of dev). | **D83 set the RoR-target loop as the post-FC redesign (replacing the fixed-floor); D87 records that its FIXED-band form is wrong on real hardware and the redesign target is a declining-RoR taper + ceiling-guard drop. This is the payoff of gating #460 on a supervised hardware roast (D82/#460): a closed control loop can only be validated on a real trajectory, and the A/B caught the flaw before it ever became the default. The advisor's post-FC judgment out-performed the deterministic loop here, which nuances the 'advisor out of the loop' thesis (D35) — for the post-FC TAPER, judgment beat the fixed rule; the deterministic win is still the DROP anchor + the safety box, not the heat curve. Cross-refs D82/D83 (#405 design this revises), D35 (advisor-out-of-loop, nuanced), #460 (stays off), #405 (full brief), the roast-7/8 over-brake findings, memories `roast9-10-postfc-ab-hardware`, `issue-405-postfc-redesign`, `operator-working-n8n-roast-system`. |
| D88 | **#405 post-FC control law v2 (implements D87, supersedes D83's law): a MEASURED-ANCHOR declining-RoR taper with a never-add-heat clamp, plus a SEPARATELY-FLAGGED 196 °C ceiling-guard drop. Adversarially ratified by safety-reviewer (7 amendments folded verbatim). Both flags ship default OFF; only the supervised hardware roast can validate (a closed loop changes the trajectory — replay cannot).** (9 Jul 2026, lead spec + safety-reviewer ratification; evidence `docs/analysis/2026-07-09-roast9-10-postfc-ab.md`) | **The law:** (1) Engagement unchanged — the true FC edge → DEVELOPMENT sets `_post_fc_engaged`; capture `ror_engage` (EMA-smoothed RoR), `heat_engage` (actuated heat), `t_engage`. (2) **Setpoint taper:** r(t) linear from r0 to `taper_end_ror_c_per_min` over `taper_duration_seconds`, held at the end value after; **r0 = clamp(ror_engage, taper_end, taper_start_max)** — anchored to the MEASURED engagement RoR, capped at the start-max, and FLOORED at the end value (a degenerate low/negative engagement RoR starts the setpoint at the end value, never below it — else the loop over-cuts on tick 1). Defaults: start-cap 8.0, end 4.0, duration 90 s (measured baseline decay 6.1→4.0 °C/min over 58–83 s; **n=2 — parameters are hardware-TUNED at the validation roast, never offline-validated**). (3) **Output law:** the existing PI (kp/ki, conditional-integration anti-windup, bumpless) drives heat toward r(t), clamped to [effective_floor, effective_ceiling] with **effective_ceiling = max(1, min(heat_ceiling, heat_engage))** — never-add-heat-beyond-entry is LAW not a knob, but the 1 % anti-stall floor WINS over it (a 0-heat FC handoff must not pin the whole development at 0); effective_floor = min(heat_floor, effective_ceiling). Monotone-decreasing-only output REJECTED (cannot recover from an over-cut → stall). Bumpless transfer is exact only when r0 == ror_engage; when r0 is clamped the documented tick-1 intent is a gentle brake. (4) **The taper clock advances on the ACTUATION clock** (the `_post_fc_last_actuation` discipline): a paused/HOLD/rate-limited stretch does NOT march the setpoint down. *(Implementation amendment, PR #490 — Codex catch:* the per-compute dt is additionally CAPPED at one control interval, `effective_dt = min(dt, control_interval_seconds)`, applied to BOTH the taper clock and the PI integrator — otherwise a gap-RESUME swallows the whole pause into one giant step: a 60 s gap stepped the integrator 72→60 % in a single tick. The actuation clock alone stops the setpoint marching *during* the gap; the dt cap stops the *catch-up* step at the end of it.)* (5) Fan unchanged — the single pinned knob; actuated fan out of scope (baseline evidence: the advisor's brake used fan 50→100; the validation roast may trial a higher pinned value). (6) **Ceiling-guard drop, DECOUPLED (safety anchor, not a taper feature):** new `ceiling_guard_drop_enabled` (own bool) + `ceiling_guard_temp_c` (default 196.0). Fires in DEVELOPMENT whenever bean ≥ guard — REGARDLESS of the RoR-loop flag or `_post_fc_engaged` (a taper-gated guard would leave every flag-OFF roast and every post-recovery resume with no deterministic bitter protection: 198 is a 2 °C margin against a measured +12 °C post-drop coast) — through `evaluate_drop_recommendation` + the executor (the same safety path as every drop). Default **False** this PR (byte-for-byte incumbent behavior); **the operator's stated intent is to flip it True at the validation roast** — a CONSCIOUS incumbent-behavior change (the advisor no longer owns the 196 boundary alone), never a silent rider. Cross-field validator (BLOCKER): `ceiling_guard_temp_c < emergency_drop_temp_c` AND `≤ bitter_ceiling_temp_c`. The drop reason is a TYPED event kind/reason — never a bare string compared in core logic (D15). (7) The deterministic anchor (bean ≥ target AND dev ≥ target) and the advisor's drop-earlier-only authority are UNCHANGED — the trace shows that authority ended tonight's runaway (advisor drop at 194 °C / 16.1 %). Resume/restart: engagement re-capture happens ONLY on a true FC edge (a recovery-resume leaves the loop inert — restart-never-auto-resumes HELD); taper state (r0, t_engage) lives in the PI snapshot/restore and clears on disengage. (8) Config: REMOVE `target_ror_c_per_min`; ADD `taper_start_max_ror_c_per_min=8.0`, `taper_end_ror_c_per_min=4.0` (validator ≤ start-cap), `taper_duration_seconds=90` (>0), `ceiling_guard_drop_enabled=False`, `ceiling_guard_temp_c=196.0`. Env/file only; config-UI exposure is a follow-up. Implementation: two thin slices — (i) the taper law, (ii) the decoupled ceiling guard. | **Why:** the ratifier walked the measured roast-2 numbers through the law: r0 = min-clamp(6.1) = 6.1 (NOT the fixed 8.0 that made D83 read 'too slow' from tick one), tick-1 error = 0 → hold at the bumpless 72 %, effective_ceiling = 72 → the observed 72→91 % monotonic climb is STRUCTURALLY impossible; and in the opposite (stall) case the loop recovers within [floor, 72] rather than staying cut — both of D87's opposite-direction failures are closed by construction. The amendments are the review's value: the guard decoupling (A1) turns a taper feature into the bitter-line safety anchor the incumbent system lacks; the separate guard flag (A2) makes the incumbent-behavior change conscious; the r0 END-value floor (B1) kills a tick-1 over-cut on a degenerate FC detection; the 1 % anti-stall ceiling floor (B2) resolves the never-add-heat vs anti-stall conflict explicitly; the actuation-clock taper (C1) stops a paused stretch from silently marching the setpoint; the FC-edge-only re-capture (C2) preserves restart-never-auto-resumes. All invariants held: controller owns the loop, every write through safety policy (the box built from ACTUATED output — told==enforced, the #412 lesson), Celsius, plain Enums. Cross-refs: D87 (the design direction this implements), D83 (the law this supersedes), #405/#460, `docs/analysis/2026-07-09-roast9-10-postfc-ab.md` (the measured evidence incl. the corrected record: roast 2's drop was ADVISOR-triggered at 194 °C / 16.51 % — the earlier-only authority worked), memories `roast9-10-postfc-ab-hardware`, `issue-405-postfc-redesign`. |
| D89 | **Merged post-FC control (operator-ratified direction, 11 Jul): the LLM and the deterministic loop CO-OPERATE — division by lever now, bounded setpoint-shaping next. "We do not pick one over the other."** (11 Jul 2026, operator + lead; evidence = the D88 validation A/B, runs `d55b0fce`/`edbe9a76`) | **Context:** the D88 taper PASSED its first hardware run (heat anchored at 65 % engagement value, never rose, trace-verified: measured RoR sat at/below the declining setpoint so the never-add-heat ceiling was the binding constraint — benign; drop 194 °C @ 13.4 % vs baseline 190 °C @ 15.0 % — the convergence thesis held). The same session showed the advisor's *judgment* was directionally right all day while three gaps surfaced: it is blind to actuated levers (agent#497 — its context carries no actuated heat/fan, so in loop mode it reasoned from an imagined heat-0), the loop-mode pinned fan wastes the second brake lever (agent#498 — actuated fan sat at the pre-FC 40 all development while the advisor's sensible 60→90 ramp was advisory-only), and drop decisions are first-past-the-post on whichever target arrives first (agent#499 — baseline sacrificed temp for DTR, treatment sacrificed DTR for temp). **The direction, two tiers:** **Tier 1 — division by lever** (implements via agent#497→#498→#499, in that order): heat stays deterministic (the D88 taper), fan returns to the advisor as an ACTUATED lever in loop mode (revises D88(5)'s pinned-fan; every fan write still through safety policy), drop stays shared (deterministic anchor + ceiling guard + advisor earlier-only), and the advisor's context gains the actuated heat/fan values plus an explicit advisory-only note for levers it does not hold. **Tier 2 — bounded setpoint-shaping** (own D-note + safety-reviewer ratification before any implementation, hardware-gated per the replay-vs-closed-loop boundary): the advisor may nudge the taper's RoR setpoint r(t) within HARD bounds (starting proposal ±1.5 °C/min), never the actuator — the PI computes heat, never-add-heat and the 196 guard bound everything, so the D87 runaway AND the hard-0 over-brake both stay impossible by construction. **Explicitly rejected:** an advisor heat-delta on the PI output (two controllers fighting one actuator at one timescale). | **Why:** D87 proved both extremes fail alone; the A/B proved the taper spine works AND that the advisor's shaping judgment has value the pinned-fan design discards. The setpoint is the clean interface between judgment and actuation — the literature pack's consensus pattern (LLM proposes a spec, the deterministic layer computes the actuator value; cascade quality-outer-loop precedent) and the natural evolution of `RoastDecision`, which already works this way for the drop. Invariants untouched: controller owns the loop, the advisor never gets write tools, every write through safety policy, restart never auto-resumes. Sequencing: Tier 1 after the roast-11/12 tasting informs how much authority the advisor earns; Tier 2 ratified separately. Cross-refs: D87/D88 (the law this shapes), D59 (read-outs stay read-outs — the advisor, not the operator, gets the fan lever), agent#497/#498/#499, memory `roast11-12-d88-validation-ab`. |

| D92 | **#525 stale-session clear: the issue's "MCP-state gate" requirement is REPLACED by a shared-DB write-recency liveness gate** (14 Jul 2026, lead + Opus safety-reviewer, design-stage; issue #525 comment has the full ruling) | The operator action to clear/end a stale roast session ships as a PURE STORE WRITE (zero MCP/roaster commands, pinned by a zero-MCP-calls regression test) behind three composed guards: (a) not the answering process's own active run → 409; (b) `completed_at_utc IS NULL` enforced atomically in the UPDATE's WHERE with rowcount check; (c) **telemetry write-recency** — refuse (409) any run with a `telemetry_snapshots.recorded_at_utc` row newer than `max(20 s, 4× telemetry_log_interval_seconds)`, evaluated in the same statement. The issue's literal requirement ("MCP-state gate: never clear while the machine could be hot; fail closed on unknown hardware state") is replaced because the answering process's own MCP child structurally cannot be the process that owned an orphaned run — guard (a) already excludes own-runs — so an MCP-idle query is either a no-op or actively misleading, and would spuriously block clearing an unrelated prior-session row during a legitimate live roast. Write-recency IS the honest cross-process hardware signal: a live controller commits telemetry every tick (from tick 1, verified — `tick_once` persists unconditionally and the first row of a run always writes), so an actively-driven run refuses within seconds regardless of which process answers. Physical hot-machine verification is explicitly punted to the operator (e-stop / direct check) in the confirm copy — no software gate can observe an orphan's roaster, and the action's power is capped at "stop the DB row from blocking a new start". | **Why recorded:** (1) the safety review's adversarial pass found the kill chain the naive design missed — `health.active_run_id` derives from `store.active_run()` (newest unfinalised row DB-wide, NOT the in-memory pointer), so a live roast shadowed by a newer stray row reads as stale to the UI, and clearing it would abort a ticking controller AND remove its API emergency-stop route (finalized runs 410 all operator actions); guard (c) closes exactly this. (2) Design-stage verification also established that a SAME-process bare orphan is unreachable by construction (`start_roast`'s critical section keeps `active_run()` and the in-memory pointer identical; `recover_on_start` funnels every non-terminal row into faulted-finalize or `operator_recovery_required` before any request is served) — the stale-session card and this action are inherently MULTI-process tools, which is load-bearing context for future reviews. (3) Deferred hardening noted, not required: a durable per-run owner marker (the dead `mcp_session_id` column carrying the #516 `instance_id` at `create_run`). Cross-refs: #525 (issue + ruling comment), #523 S4 (the stale card), #516/D-instance-id (health identity), #513 (the incident class), D15 (typed verdicts — untouched; this action issues no roaster write), memory `port-8000-impostor-incident`. |
| D93 | **E11-S1 (#137) split: wheel/SPA-bundling ships now; the `[pi]` extra defers to a follow-up story, still blocked on the D27 torch-free MCP gate** (14 Jul 2026, engineer + team-lead, implementation) | **Resolves open item 3** (hatchling build-hook approach): a custom hatchling build hook (`hatch_build.py`) runs `npm ci && npm run build` in `web/` and force-includes the result into the wheel at `roastpilot_agent/_web_dist` — **only for a standard (non-editable) build**; `pip install -e .` skips the hook entirely and keeps resolving the source-checkout `web/dist` (no forced Node dependency on the normal dev loop). `live.default_spa_dir()` now tries the bundled package data (via `importlib.resources`, requiring a real filesystem `Path` — the pip-install-as-directory case, which is what pip always does) before falling back to the source-checkout path. A CI `package` job builds the real wheel and smoke-tests it in a clean venv (CLI `--help`/`--version` + a replay-mode HTTP fetch of the served `index.html`, non-8000 port); `tests/test_packaging.py` proves the same end to end (builds the wheel, installs into an isolated venv, asserts the resolved SPA dir is inside that venv's site-packages, never the source checkout). **The `pi` optional-dependency extra is explicitly NOT shipped in this story, and NOT even scaffolded** (lead ruling): its prerequisite, D27 Phase 2 (`coffee-roaster-mcp#157`, dropping `torch`/`transformers`), is still open — a direct PyPI check found the current release (0.1.13) still hard-requires `transformers`. The epic file (`docs/epics/E11-packaging.md`) already said not to pin/ship the `[pi]` extra pre-Phase-2, so shipping it — or even a TODO-commented scaffold — against a torch-heavy MCP release would both violate that gate and publish an installable `pip install roastpilot-agent[pi]` that mislabels itself to operators expecting a lean Pi appliance set. **Verified the base wheel itself stays lean in the meantime:** the shipped wheel's `Requires-Dist` has no `coffee-roaster-mcp`/`transformers`/`torch` (confirmed by inspecting the built wheel's METADATA and by a clean-venv install's `pip list`) — `coffee-roaster-mcp` is a dev-group-only pin (tests spawn it in mock-driver mode) and never leaks into the runtime dependency set of the shipped artifact. Split into a follow-up story tracked in the epic's status table; the PR uses `Refs #137`/`Part of #137`, not `Closes #137`, so #137 stays open until the `[pi]`-extra half ships. | **Why:** finishing the unblocked half now (SPA bundling has no cross-repo dependency) rather than waiting idle on `#157` keeps the story moving without overclaiming the `[pi]` extra's acceptance criterion — the plan repo's own accuracy boundaries (no "production-ready" ahead of validation) extend naturally to "don't ship an extra pretending to be torch-free when it isn't." The arm64 smoke (originally an S1 criterion) is deferred alongside the `[pi]` extra since it verifies Pi-only deps that do not exist yet in the base wheel. Cross-refs: `docs/epics/E11-packaging.md` (S1 acceptance criteria + status table, updated in the same PR), D27 (the torch-free gate this still respects), D28 (the now-cleared manual-test gate that unblocked S1/S2 scaffolding), issue `coffee-roaster-mcp#157`. |
| D94 | **#521 RATIFIED: the post-FC taper's setpoint anchor moves from measured-RoR to AFFORDABILITY — cut only when measured RoR exceeds `(target_drop_temp − bean_temp) / remaining_dwell_budget` (lag-adjusted), decisiveness margin k = 1.5 °C/min; headroom-threshold laws REJECTED** (14 Jul 2026, operator tasting verdict + lead; ratification on issue #521) | The tasting gate answered: BOTH weekend roasts cupped "a bit flat" — roast 13 via temperature underdevelopment (dropped 190 °C, 5 °C short, off an early-mark-inflated DTR clock) and roast 14 via time underdevelopment (DTR 15.1 % vs 17 %, heat never cut). Offline trace replay (all four D88-era roasts) established two facts that reshape the law: (1) **the D88 taper never issued a single heat cut in any roast** — its measured-RoR anchor tracks the naturally-falling post-FC RoR inside the ±1.0 deadband for the whole dwell, so D88's validated success was the HOLD at heat_engage + the never-add-heat ceiling, not active tapering; (2) **headroom cannot discriminate** — roasts 12 (9/10 cup) and 14 (flat) had identical 9 °C headroom and heat_engage; any threshold firing on 14 would have trimmed the programme's best roast. The affordability anchor separates them by what actually differs: whether the measured RoR exceeds what the remaining temperature-and-time budget can absorb. Replay: decisive 58→52-class cut on roast 14, near-no-op on roast 12. **AMENDED (14 Jul, same day, operator + safety review — pre-implementation):** the comparison gains a fixed hysteresis — cut iff `measured_ror > affordable + m`, `m = 0.1 °C/min` (config `affordability_hysteresis_c_per_min`, ge=0, default 0.1) — because store-exact literals showed roast 12's no-op margin was 0.08 °C/min, NARROWER than FC clock noise (±5 s of C-origin error ≈ 0.081 °C/min): without m the validated-roast decision is clock-noise-determined. Simulation: m=0.1 keeps roast 12 a no-op across ±5 s and target 14.5-15 % while roast 14 still cuts across its whole range; m≥0.2 wrongly spares roast 14. COUPLED spec line: `C = _first_crack_monotonic − _charge_monotonic` (both agent-monotonic, honouring the #337 backdate; read after the FC edge sets it) — the receive-tick clock would spend ~half the hysteresis budget on avoidable origin error. Scope: shapes ONLY the initial setpoint — never-add-heat ceiling, 196 guard, D88 flags, actuator authority byte-unchanged; rides inside the existing post_first_crack_control.enabled gate. **Part 2 (FC-time-aware drop-window DTR weighting — roast 13's fix) folds into #499's joint-objective follow-through, a separate slice.** #380 (MCP-owned FC-lag offset) stays complementary: it shrinks the mark error, this law governs the loop's response to whatever clock it gets; re-tune k down if #380 ships. | **Why:** the flat-cup pair is the first tasting evidence that both FC-mark-error failure modes produce real quality loss, and the replay evidence refutes the issue's own original proposal (headroom scaling) before a line of code — a #346/D56-class prevented-wrong-build, this time by analysis. Process: design pass + safety review precede implementation per the issue mandate; D94 records the ratified direction, the design pass details it. Cross-refs: D88 (the taper this revises — its hold+ceiling behavior is retained), D90 (roast-12 validation the law must not regress), #499 (part 2's home), #380 (complementary clock fix), issue #521 (evidence + ratification thread), memory `batch3-safety-arc-and-e11-kickoff`. |
| D95 | **D94 SUPERSEDED — the affordability-anchored post-FC setpoint is FALSIFIED pre-merge; PR #551 closed unmerged; D88's validated hold+ceiling stack stands** (15 Jul 2026, operator ruling on an independent safety-review verdict; full record on PR #551 + issue #521) | The falsification, verified independently against the real store and the code: (1) the law's remaining-dwell budget omitted the time already elapsed at the decision instant — a near-CONSTANT ~22.0 s across all four D88-era roasts (the FC detector's 20 s confirmation window + processing; measured 21.992-22.017 s), because development starts at the crack ONSET while the decision runs at the receive tick; (2) with the term added, the law fires on NOTHING (roast 14, the only validating cut, flips to no-op at margin −1.33; roasts 11/12/13 no-op at −2.9 to −10.2) — the ratified 12-vs-14 discrimination was a <0.4 °C/min artifact of the omitted term cancelling the 60 s-window RoR's measurement lag; (3) no corrected form is constructible with current instrumentation: the MCP exposes only the 60 s-window `bean_ror_c_per_min` (no shorter-window field exists in the schema), the two roasts' pre-crack ramps converge (6.15 vs 6.10 °C/min), and their REALIZED drop signatures are identical (both <195 °C, both under target DTR) with opposite cups — the quality discriminator lives in the BEAN (El Durazno white honey 9/10 vs Sumatra wet-hulled flat), outside every variable the law reads; (4) re-tuning constants under corrected physics requires a NEGATIVE hysteresis (fires the "too fast" cut when the roast is SLOWER than affordable) — n=2 curve-fitting with inverted physics, rejected on the rigour bar. **Kept:** D88's hold-at-engagement + never-add-heat + 196 ceiling guard, byte-unchanged and tasting-validated (roast 12's 9/10 was a D94 no-op case). **Unaffected and proceeding:** #499 part 2 (FC-time-aware drop-window DTR weighting — the roast-13 fix; mechanism keys on the DTR clock + ceiling guard, never on the falsified seed law; confirmed by code inspection). **Future enablers logged:** an MCP short-window RoR field (coffee-roaster-mcp issue; would make a corrected affordability law testable), #380 (MCP-owned FC-lag offset — shrinks the ~22 s error at the source; unaffected by this falsification and MORE motivated by it). | **Why recorded:** D94 was ratified, amended, twice safety-reviewed, and fully implemented (10-case test matrix, all gates green) — and was still WRONG, caught one gate before hardware by a fresh-context diff review whose physics question (Codex P2: "subtract the FC backdate from the remaining budget") survived every refutation attempt. The layered process worked exactly as designed at its most expensive-but-correct point: the sunk implementation (PR #551's branch record, preserved) is the price of stopping a heat-cut law near the bitter ceiling whose stated mechanism was false. Two durable lessons: (a) validate control-law arithmetic against the CLOCK SEMANTICS of its inputs (onset-referenced budgets vs receive-tick decisions) before ratifying — the knife-edge margins (±0.08-0.36 °C/min) should have been the tell that the discrimination was noise-scale; (b) a law whose validating evidence separates by <0.5 °C/min on n=2 roasts of DIFFERENT beans is fitting bean identity, not control physics — bean-aware laws need bean-aware inputs (the E14/D42 corpus trajectory, not the controller). Cross-refs: D94 (superseded), D88/D90 (the standing validated stack), #499 (part 2 home), #380, #521 + PR #551 (the full falsification record), memory `batch3-safety-arc-and-e11-kickoff`. |
| D96 | **Post-FC heat becomes BOUNDED-BIDIRECTIONAL (relaxing D88's never-add-heat-beyond-entry clamp under a recovery law) + fan stays a first-class advisor control lever with coupling-aware teaching + bottom-edge target-seeking drop teaching** (15 Jul 2026, operator: "Heat can go either way, fan is valuable and should be used to control"; evidence issue #559, roast 15 run 8ac8a5e4) | Roast 15 exposed a lever conflict the D88 box cannot resolve: the advisor pushed fan 30→90 in the first development minute (its one actuated lever, used for temperature control), convectively crashing RoR 7→3 °C/min; the taper saw measured RoR fall 1.5-2 °C/min below its declining setpoint — the textbook raise condition — but heat sat AT the never-add-heat ceiling (entry=60 via the trim-60 recipe), so the loop had ZERO raise authority; the bean crawled 183→188 over 115 s and the advisor dropped at the DTR window BOTTOM (16.3 vs target 19) citing "in range" at 188 °C — 7 °C short (roast-13 boundary-seeking at the opposite edge; c7/PR #554 guards only the top edge). Ratified scope: (1) the loop may raise heat ABOVE entry when measured RoR runs persistently below setpoint, inside a HARD cap + the existing slew/deadband structure + the 196 guard — the design pass must formalize why runaway (roast-9 class) stays structurally impossible: the setpoint is measured-anchored and DECAYING (unlike roast 9's fixed band that sat above measured forever), and the cap bounds worst-case authority; (2) advisor fan authority unchanged but the teaching carries the fan→RoR coupling explicitly, and the deferred advisor-visibility item RETURNS to scope (the advisor should see the loop's heat state/authority so the two levers stop fighting blind); (3) target-seeking drop teaching for the bottom edge (aim target_development_percent, never treat the earliest legal point as the target). | **Why:** the trim-60 + dev-19 recipe moved every number the right way on roast 15 (FC 183 vs 186, dev 115 s vs 83, DTR 16.5 vs 15.1; the dev-19 floor correctly blocked sub-16 drops) — the remaining failure is the LEVER ARCHITECTURE: one lever crashes RoR while the other is forbidden to answer. D88's asymmetric box was the right first posture (it ended the roast-9 runaway era and produced the 9/10 cup) but roast 15 shows the asymmetry binds exactly when the advisor uses fan as intended. D95's lessons bind the design pass: no knife-edge arithmetic discriminators, clock-semantics validation, hardware gate before any default change. **AMENDED (15 Jul, same day — design-review conditions folded):** (1) exit semantics specified — exit hysteresis uses a SEPARATE smaller margin (`recovery_exit_margin_c_per_min` < trigger margin, validator-enforced) held for the same confirm-ticks, and on exit the effective CEILING glides down at `recovery_exit_glide_pp_per_tick` (default 5) to heat_engage — fast entry / slow exit by design (anti-limit-cycle), with a limit-cycle convergence test mandatory; (2) **guard-coupling validator: `recovery_enabled=True` REQUIRES `ceiling_guard_drop_enabled=True`** — the review traced every heat-command path and found the 196 line is protected ONLY by the guard drop (safety.py's command path is temp-blind; telemetry cuts only at 230), and a config comment falsely claiming 196/198 output clamping must be corrected; (3) structural fan-cap-during-recovery DEFERRED by lead ruling — the two-lever worst case (fan 100 vs heat entry+15) fails COLD (quality, not safety: the raise is defeated, never a bitter approach; the 196 guard is lever-agnostic), the operator ratified fan as first-class, and the heat cap does not stack across cycles; recorded as a named fallback (`recovery-active fan ceiling`) whose trigger is hardware evidence of fan defeating recovery on a validation roast. Cross-refs: D88 (the clamp being relaxed — its floor/anti-stall/ceiling-guard structure is retained), D89/#498 (the coalesced fan write), D94/D95 (the falsified sibling law + its lessons), #499/c7 (top-edge teaching this completes), #497 (actuated-lever context), issue #559 (evidence + ratification). |
| D102 | **Learned plant model → predictive (MPC) post-FC control — a staged, data-gated experiment; operationalises the process-model half of the D42 learning loop** (18 Jul 2026, operator + lead; detailed plan `roastpilot-agent/plant-model-mpc-plan.md`; tracking issue roastpilot-agent#580) | Build a learned FORWARD model of the roaster's thermal response (heat/fan + recent history → bean RoR at t+20/30/40 s, past the ~25-35 s TC lag) and use it for anticipatory (MPC) post-FC control — the projection the system lacks today (only the pre-FC FC-ETA #229 projects RoR; the D88 taper is reactive, the drop anchor threshold-based, the c3 advisor non-projective). Motivating evidence: the 18 Jul Brazil-Santos baseline roast dropped ~7 °C early on a FELT trajectory (advisor claimed "reached 195 °C" at bean 188). **Data (pinned in the plan file):** two corpora, ONE plant (operator-confirmed same Hottop + room) — 47 Artisan `.alog` (BT + ET both real, + heat/fan control events, ~41.5k samples, verified) + 14 completed store roasts (full telemetry + Yocto ambient; the store's other runs are faulted/aborted), = ~61 roasts / ~35k usable modelling ticks at 1 Hz; future durable feature store = Snowflake `roast_telemetry` (D97, live via D99). **Phases (cheap-first — complexity follows the corpus):** 0 data foundation (Snowflake) → **1 ARX feasibility (RUNNING 18 Jul:** multi-horizon RoR-vs-baseline + heat-step counterfactual under leave-one-roast-out CV**)** → 2 grey-box + FC-exotherm regime + bean/ambient features + uncertainty → 3 deep learning only when data-earned → 4 MPC in sim → 5 supervised hardware A/B. Distinct from #567 (parked same day): that learned a bean-SPECIFIC outcome shape (inert/fragile on a small bean-varying corpus); this learns bean-INVARIANT machine physics, which transfers cross-session — the reason pooling the corpora is defensible. | **Why:** the projection gap is the crucial control lever (the operator's own diagnosis, confirmed by the early-drop roast), and the ~25-35 s TC + ~12-21 s FC-audio lag make current-RoR stale — projection is the only principled compensation. It is the process model D42 §2.3 always named as MPC's prerequisite, now made concrete on data that already exists. Guardrails (non-negotiable): the controller owns the loop; the MPC is DETERMINISTIC controller code (a D88-taper upgrade path); the advisor stays advisory-only (never handed the model); every roaster write still clamps through safety policy; ship-disabled + hardware-gated like D88/D96; Phase-4/5 routed through `safety-reviewer`. Risks the phases explicitly gate: calibration alignment (Artisan↔MCP BT — resolved empirically at Phase 1 step 1, not assumed), EXCITATION (smooth roasts teach the model little — checked first), the FC-exotherm regime change, and data volume capping model class (ARX/grey-box now, DL only when earned — a perfect small-set score is a warning). Ambient handled per the plan: a weak second-order covariate, tested MEASURED on the 26 that have it before any historical-weather backfill of the 47 (indoor≠outdoor is lossy; pressure backfills cleanly, temp/RH are heating-confounded). Cross-refs: D42/`ml-learning-loop-plan.md` (its §2.3 process-model + §7.1 target-curve halves — this is the former), D88/D96 (the reactive taper/recovery an MPC would eventually wrap or supersede), #229 (the one existing RoR projection), #342 (ambient probe), #567 (the parked LLM-side counterpart), D97/D99/D101 (the Snowflake substrate + M2 telemetry sync), #380 + coffee-roaster-mcp short-window-RoR (enablers that sharpen the inputs). |
| D103 | **Claude outage uses a reversible operator exception; the SHA-scoped review-gate suspension experiment is rejected, and restoration requires a PR-scoped primitive** (25 Jul 2026, operator + Codex engineer; roastpilot-agent#663) | During the operator-authorized Claude Code Review outage, remove only `review-gate` from `main`'s required status contexts. Keep the three app-pinned CI checks, app-pinned `codecov/patch`, strict mode, `required_conversation_resolution`, and `enforce_admins`; Codecov ingestion recovered and its patch gate was restored on 26 Jul via #646. Claude is optional and must not delay delivery, but every PR still requires applicable shift-left domain reviewers, full local gates, the authenticated exact-head Codex trigger/wait/triage protocol (including its documented bounded silent/stalled fallback), and independent lead/`pr-triage` adjudication. Draft PR #662 is closed unmerged: its exact suspension rerun binding was sound, but the surrounding commit-status protocol could not safely prove PR-specific or ready-generation review coverage. Restoration is tracked by #663 and must use a genuinely PR-scoped merge primitive; never restore automation by re-requiring the known-unsafe SHA-scoped status. | **Why:** GitHub commit statuses are keyed by SHA/context, while inline findings, draft/ready generations, and conversation resolution are PR-scoped. Two PRs can share a SHA, a prior draft success can remain visible before an Actions job reacts to `ready_for_review`, and `concurrency.queue: max` does not guarantee dispatch order. Shift-left security, QA, and independent triage caught these fail-open cases before #662 merged. The temporary exception is narrower and honest: it changes one required context, records the substitute manual gates, preserves the rest of branch protection, and has an explicit restoration issue with same-SHA/different-PR and draft→ready acceptance tests. |
| D104 | **MCP stop cancellation retains ownership until the child owner is cancelled and bounded-reaped; test-only parse workers are explicitly shut down** (25 Jul 2026, Codex engineer + QA; roastpilot-agent#665) | If the task awaiting `MCPServerProcess.stop()` is itself cancelled, `stop()` keeps the existing fail-closed order (mark unconfirmed and force-terminate first), then cancels and bounded-reaps the captured owner task before re-raising `CancelledError`; it never clears the only owner reference while teardown is still live. Tests that restart a process own the second start through a final stop. The test harness explicitly shuts down its bounded bean-sourcing parse workers before interpreter exit and carries a fresh-subprocess exit regression that exercises parse-pool use plus a real mock-driver MCP start/stop. This test cleanup is not a claim that production can kill a genuinely stuck Python thread: if the regression proves an unbounded parser, process isolation is a separate story. | **Why:** three documentation-only PR runs on unchanged `main` printed `3195 passed, 3 skipped` and then failed to exit. QA confirmed two leaked `_run_session` tasks, including the safety-relevant production cancellation path, and observed live non-daemon parse-pool threads after targeted tests. Redirecting stderr, pinning a runner image, or blindly retrying would hide ownership defects while leaving shutdown behavior uncertain. The fix preserves restart-to-recovery semantics and every write/safety boundary; it only makes teardown ownership explicit and test exit deterministic. |
| D105 | **#665 clean-exit diagnosis AMENDS D104: completed MCP generations are stop-finalized only, and the replay CLI retains cleanup ownership outside Uvicorn lifespan** (25 Jul 2026, Codex engineer + independent safety/QA/PR triage) | An MCP owner that exits unexpectedly fails closed before its context unwinds, while its generation's PID hook is current; `start()` never drains or signals a retained owner, whether pending or completed, and refuses replacement until `stop()` finalizes that identity. Completed owner outcomes are drain-only because a later PID action may target a recycled process. Separately, `_serve_replay` owns `ReplaySource` cleanup after successful creation even though the app lifespan also closes it: an early/no-op/failing Uvicorn serve path cancels and drains a pending server task, then idempotently closes the source from a nested `finally`, preserving the primary replay error. | **Why:** exact-head Codex review found the stale-PID and replacement-generation gaps in D104's first implementation. An unprivileged Linux full-suite trace then proved the remaining post-summary hang was three live `aiosqlite` workers created by replay CLI paths whose mocked/early Uvicorn return never entered lifespan; the parse pool was no longer live. The final Linux suite returned with only `MainThread`, and GitHub CI proceeded past pytest through contract drift, coverage upload, and CLI smoke. This broadens the ownership diagnosis without changing any roaster write, safety verdict, restart-to-recovery, or advisor-authority boundary. |
| D106 | **Live CLI finalization is process-bounded only AFTER its ordered safety teardown; first SIGINT preserves teardown, second SIGINT is an explicit immediate forced abort** (26 Jul 2026, operator + Codex engineer; roastpilot-agent#667) | Every established live service retains the load-bearing shutdown order: heat-off attempt → service stop → MCP stop/force-termination → unconfirmed-stop evidence → store close. The first SIGINT may cancel the live coroutine only after that five-step teardown is run in a separately-owned, cancellation-resistant task; cancellation then propagates normally. Once teardown closes the store, a pre-started daemon watchdog arms for a fixed code-owned 10 seconds around `asyncio.run()`'s residual-task cancellation/gather, `shutdown_asyncgens()`, and default-executor shutdown. Normal return/raise disarms it; expiry identifies snapshotted residual tasks, writes a low-level diagnostic, and terminates with stable nonzero exit code 70. It applies after every established live teardown, not only a known retained MCP owner. The owner reference is never cleared or replaced because the bound expired. A second SIGINT remains the operator's immediate escape: it warns that teardown may be interrupted and hardware state is uncertain, then forces abnormal exit rather than claiming the five-step guarantee. | **Why:** D104/D105 bound MCP ownership operations but Python 3.11's top-level `asyncio.run()` still gathers cancellation-resistant tasks and joins the default executor without a timeout. Python cannot forcibly finish an arbitrary coroutine or thread, so the honest boundary is the OS process. Arming only after store close prevents ordinary residual cleanup from bypassing heat-off/evidence, while the explicit second-interrupt exception preserves an escape from a wedged teardown instead of hiding that tradeoff. The 10-second bound is deliberately independent of operator-configurable MCP stop timing. Scope limits are explicit: it does not bound Uvicorn's pre-teardown graceful wait, a non-default executor/atexit hook after `asyncio.run()` returns, or native code monopolizing the GIL. Fresh-process tests must prove ordered durable evidence, bounded abnormal exit, residual-owner identification, and no surviving child PID. |
| D107 | **Saved configuration activates at the next-roast boundary; an active roast remains frozen, and bean drafting never activates saved settings early** (26 Jul 2026, operator ruling; roastpilot-agent#596) | `PUT /api/config` persists and reports the edited values immediately, but runtime configuration is replaced only by the existing between-roast reload inside `start_roast`. A roast already in progress continues with the advisor, controller, safety policy, and device configuration it started with. The draft-from-URL path uses the service's currently activated `AdvisorConfig` and `BeanSourcingConfig`; it does not call `load_app_config`, rebuild an advisor, or otherwise make a saved edit effective before the next roast. Drafting remains unavailable while a roast is active, so it cannot contend with or mutate the active loop. After the next roast boundary activates the saved configuration, later drafts naturally use that newly activated service configuration. | **Why:** the operator expects every runtime setting change to have one predictable activation point: the next roast. Immediate draft-only freshness would create a second activation model where the same saved provider/model setting affects an offline LLM call before it affects roasting, while an active roast must remain immutable. Keeping one boundary makes UI persistence distinct from runtime activation and preserves the safety-critical frozen-run contract. Cross-refs: D76/D78/D79 (config architecture and apply-next-roast reload), #430/#431 (agent/device activation), #587/#657 (draft isolation and roast-start preemption), #596. |
| D108 | **Claude review becomes a required, PR-scoped approval produced only by a trusted default-branch bridge after the newest exact-PR/head review succeeds; supersedes D58 and completes D103's restoration design** (27 Jul 2026, operator + Codex engineer; roastpilot-agent#663) | The raw `claude-review` check remains non-required because workflow-editing and Dependabot PRs cannot run it reliably. Instead, a trusted `workflow_run` bridge maps the newest successful `Claude Code Review` run for the exact PR number, head and base refs/SHAs/repositories, and run attempt to a GitHub approval on that PR. Branch protection then requires one approval and dismisses it on every code push. Before a same-head manual rerun, the operator dismisses the bridge approval and only then dispatches the rerun because webhook delivery cannot make that transition atomic; the bridge's `in_progress`, non-success, stale-inventory, and delayed-event paths are defensive revocation backstops. Inline Claude findings remain independently blocking through `required_conversation_resolution`. An exact-head Claude review performed while a PR is draft remains valid when it is marked ready or reopened without a code change, so those lifecycle events do not launch duplicate Claude reviews; any push launches a fresh review and invalidates the old approval. Codex is separate: `ready_for_review` starts a fresh Codex review even on an unchanged SHA, so ready must precede the final Codex round and only a fully-paginated, bot-authenticated exact-head verdict posted after ready can authorize merge. A first cancelled Claude attempt is rerun once, while failure, timeout, action-required, a second cancellation, missing/ambiguous PR association, stale head/base, or an unexpected workflow path all fail closed. Dependabot receives a labelled Claude approval from trusted default-branch code. A workflow-editing PR can replace the upstream reviewer workflow itself, so its apparent success is never trusted and it requires an explicit maintainer approval with the reason recorded; neither path executes PR code with the bridge token. Authenticated zero-Python diffs receive normal `codecov/patch` check-runs (#676/#678), and Dependabot #675 likewise received SUCCESS after a public-repository tokenless upload; #677's apparent permanent deadlock was a timing misdiagnosis, not an activation prerequisite. All other CI, Codecov, conversation-resolution, Codex, and independent-triage requirements remain. GitHub's approval-count rule cannot pin the approver identity, so any other human approval that satisfies it is treated as an explicit operator override and must be recorded, never as silent Claude evidence. The repository-wide Actions setting that permits GitHub Actions to approve PRs and the one-approval branch-protection rule activate only after the bridge has merged to the default branch and live adversarial proof passes. | **Why:** D58's commit-status protocol was SHA-scoped and therefore could not distinguish two PRs sharing one head commit, prove which PR generation was reviewed, or reliably sequence draft-to-ready events. GitHub reviews and stale-review dismissal are PR-scoped, so the required primitive now has the same identity boundary as the evidence it represents. Preserving a Claude draft review across a no-code lifecycle transition is an explicit policy choice: the reviewed bytes are unchanged, and removing redundant `ready_for_review` / `reopened` triggers eliminates the duplicate-run race observed in practice. That choice does not carry over to Codex's independently observed ready trigger. The bridge never interprets review prose or self-certifies findings; it proves only that the exact review run succeeded, while inline threads and independent D23 triage continue to decide whether feedback is resolved. Activation is deliberately a second slice so branch protection cannot require an approval producer that is not yet trusted on `main`. Cross-refs: D23, D37/D77/D79 (review roster), D58 (superseded SHA gate), D103 (outage and restoration constraint), roastpilot-agent#663/#677. |
| D109 | **AMENDS D108: exact PR/head/base Claude approval is monotonic for unchanged bytes; a same-head rerun is additive unless the operator dismisses approval before requesting replacement** (30 Jul 2026, operator + Codex engineer; roastpilot-agent#663/#679) | A successful bridge approval remains valid across later Claude attempts while repository, PR number, head SHA/ref/repository, and base SHA/ref/repository are unchanged. Same-head success is idempotent; same-head failure, cancellation, start, delayed delivery, or stale run inventory cannot revoke prior valid evidence. Inline findings from any later run still block through required conversation resolution, and an intentionally started review must finish and be independently triaged before merge; auto-merge is never armed while such a run is active. If an operator distrusts or intends to replace existing evidence, the explicit sequence is: record the reason, ensure auto-merge is not armed, dismiss approval, then dispatch the rerun; the PR remains unapproved until replacement success. Code pushes and base retargets still invalidate approval automatically and require fresh exact-identity review. Reopening preserves valid evidence but reruns the newest exact review when approval is absent. Dependabot exemptions are replaced only after base-retarget privileged-file recheck; workflow or privileged-helper edits require recorded maintainer approval. | **Why:** final independent triage of #679 proved that GitHub exposes no transaction joining “read newest workflow run” to “post PR approval.” Handler concurrency cannot serialize an out-of-band rerun creation: a new run can start after the final inventory read but before approval POST, briefly satisfying branch protection with evidence D108 intended to revoke. Repeated reads, delays, or cross-workflow queues only narrow that window.
