Because identical PR/head/base identity means the reviewed bytes and effective diff are unchanged, monotonic evidence is honest and removes the impossible revocation race; replacement becomes an explicit dismiss-first operator operation. This does not weaken changed-code/base invalidation, inline conversation resolution, exact-head Codex, CI/Codecov, or independent triage. |
| D110 | **AMENDS D108/D109: Claude approval binds the immutable base branch identity (repository + ref), not its moving tip SHA; the reviewed tip remains audit metadata** (31 Jul 2026, operator + Codex/security/independent triage; roastpilot-agent#663/#679) | Exact evidence binds repository, PR number, head SHA/ref/repository, and base ref/repository. The bridge records the run's base SHA for audit but does not use that moving value for validity. A base retarget still invalidates evidence and reruns review; an ordinary protected-base advance does not strand an unchanged-head completion. This is load-bearing on strict required-status mode, stale-review dismissal, admin enforcement, and disabled protected-base force-push/deletion. If those controls change, this identity design must be re-evaluated. | **Why:** GitHub snapshots `base.sha` in a workflow run but emits no PR `edited` event when the protected base merely advances. Requiring equality with the live base tip can therefore reject a successful review permanently. Under strict protection the now-behind PR cannot merge until its head is updated; that head change dismisses approval and triggers fresh review. If the head already contains the new base tip, the earlier review covered a superset of the resulting diff. Binding repo/ref preserves retarget protection without coupling liveness to a moving tip. |
| D111 | **AMENDS D108-D110: lifecycle and eventually-consistent run metadata recover automatically; approval publication is pre/post identity-checked and activation requires a non-current-commit adversarial proof** (31 Jul 2026, operator + Codex/Claude/independent triage; roastpilot-agent#663/#679) | `ready_for_review` and `reopened` each start a fresh additive Claude run; valid same-identity approval remains monotonic, while failed or missing history no longer deadlocks. A successful incoming exact run newer than stale inventory is authoritative; only a known newer run defers it. Empty `workflow_run.pull_requests` is recovered by bounded refresh of that exact run, followed by one bounded rerun when first-attempt association remains absent; repeated absence/ambiguity fails closed. Before approval POST the bridge reloads exact identity, then reloads again afterward and dismisses the created review if identity moved. Slice-2 activation must live-prove that GitHub does not count an approval bound to a non-current commit; failure blocks activation. | **Why:** exact-head Codex and Claude review found four liveness/race gaps after #679's first hardened head: stale approval could be posted after a synchronize dismissal, reopen/ready could lack review history, retry success could outrun the run-list API, and run association could be transiently empty. REST offers no atomic compare-and-approve operation, so bounded reconciliation plus pre/post checks narrow the race while platform non-current-commit semantics remain a load-bearing fact that must be proved live before enforcement. Additive lifecycle reviews are compatible with D109 because unchanged-identity success remains monotonic and all new inline findings still block through conversation resolution. |
| D112 | **AMENDS D109/D111: an explicit same-identity dismissal starts a durable evidence epoch, and failed post-publication validation must attempt exact-review cleanup** (31 Jul 2026, operator + Codex/security/independent triage; roastpilot-agent#663/#679) | Claude approval evidence records a versioned workflow/run-number/attempt order. A dismissed bridge approval for the current exact identity is a durable tombstone: a delayed completion may publish only when its order is strictly newer than every matching tombstone; missing or malformed current-identity tombstone order fails closed. This preserves ordinary additive reruns while making dismiss-first replacement real—attempt 2 may replace dismissed attempt 1, and a later run may replace an older dismissed run. After approval POST returns a review ID, any API or validation failure during the required identity reload triggers dismissal of that exact created review before the bridge fails. Cleanup failure remains loud and blocks activation pending operator audit. | **Why:** the next exact-head Codex round found two residual races. Stale run inventory could let an already-running old success recreate approval after an operator deliberately dismissed it but before the replacement appeared. Separately, a transient or malformed post-POST PR response left the created approval active while the bridge failed. Inventory alone cannot encode an operator revocation boundary, so dismissed exact-identity evidence becomes the durable epoch marker. REST still cannot make publication and cleanup transactional, so D111's slice-2 live proof remains mandatory. |
| D113 | **AMENDS D111: every normal-PR metadata edit starts a same-identity additive Claude review; only base retargeting dismisses evidence** (31 Jul 2026, operator + Codex/security/independent triage; roastpilot-agent#663/#679) | The reviewer runs for every normal-PR `edited` event. Title/body edits leave exact identity and existing approval unchanged, so their reviews are additive under D109. A base retarget still dismisses stale identity-bound evidence and requires current-base success. Dependabot is excluded by PR author rather than event actor and retains its trusted exemption path. | **Why:** GitHub creates and orders a workflow run before evaluating its job-level condition. A metadata-edit run whose only job skipped could still cancel the active per-PR review and become the bridge's newest run, permanently deferring the real success without producing a successor. Filtering terminal skipped runs is insufficient because the metadata run may be queued or in progress when cancellation and ordering occur. Running the additive review costs an extra review on metadata edits but preserves a single coherent run-order owner without weakening permissions or trust boundaries. |
| D114 | **AMENDS D108-D113: bridge reconciliation events are never concurrency-dropped, and Dependabot provenance is enforced in both event channels** (31 Jul 2026, operator + Codex/security/independent triage; roastpilot-agent#663/#679) | The bridge workflow has no Actions concurrency group because GitHub may replace a pending same-group run even with cancellation disabled. Handlers instead rely on exact identity, stale-only dismissal, run ordering, tombstones, pagination, and pre/post validation to remain safe under concurrent and out-of-order delivery. Dependabot author routing applies to both `pull_request_target` and associated reviewer `workflow_run` events: safe changes retain only labelled exemption evidence, false normal bridge approval is dismissed, and privileged changes lose all automated evidence and require explicit maintainer approval. JSON mutations declare `application/json`. | **Why:** an exact-head Codex round found that a base-retarget reconciliation could be the pending bridge run discarded by a later event, leaving old-base approval live if the new review failed. It also found that a skipped Dependabot reviewer workflow could race the exemption handler and be misrepresented as normal Claude success, and that urllib mutation bodies lacked the media type GitHub expects. Durable dispatch and author-based provenance are part of the approval security boundary; serialization that may drop events is weaker than the order-safe handlers already established by D109-D113. |
| D115 | **AMENDS D112/D114: approval publication is two-phase and retarget cleanup is scoped to the departed base identity** (31 Jul 2026, operator + exact-head Codex/security/QA/independent triage; roastpilot-agent#663/#679) | The bridge first creates a non-counting pending review, revalidates live PR identity and the dismissal epoch, then submits that known review as `APPROVE`. It revalidates both again after submission and dismisses the exact review if either changed or validation fails. Exact full-body/run pending evidence is adopted and resumed; different-run current-identity pending work is never deleted by a concurrent handler. Pending reviews are snapshotted before the live PR is reloaded; only superseded-head/base evidence in that snapshot is deleted, and the stale handler aborts so it cannot delete later-created current work. Known owned pending evidence is deleted on pre-submit identity or epoch abort. An indeterminate submit is state-checked: exact approved evidence continues to validation, while exact pending evidence is retained for explicit retry. Any validation or cleanup failure blocks activation for operator audit. Base-retarget handlers reload the live PR and dismiss or delete only approved or pending evidence whose embedded identity names `changes.base.ref.from`; delayed A-to-B delivery cannot remove current C evidence or publish an intermediate Dependabot exemption. | **Why:** direct approval creation left no review ID when the response was lost, so an active approval could survive an indeterminate failure; checking the dismissal epoch only before publication allowed a delayed old handler to recreate intentionally revoked evidence; broad or stale-snapshot pending cleanup could delete a concurrent handler's live work while superseded pending evidence could block retry; and comparing every review only with an event's intermediate target let delayed retarget delivery remove a later valid approval. Two-phase publication bounds ambiguous transport outcomes, exact-run adoption preserves concurrency ownership, post-submit epoch validation closes the observed replacement race, and live-identity superseded/departed cleanup makes reconciliation commutative for current evidence. REST remains non-transactional, so auto-merge stays off and slice-2 live adversarial proof remains mandatory before activation. |
| D116 | **AMENDS D115: retarget reconciliation is live-identity snapshot reconciliation, not departed-ref cleanup** (31 Jul 2026, operator + exact-head Codex/independent triage; roastpilot-agent#663/#679) | A retarget handler first snapshots all bridge reviews, then reloads the live PR. From only that immutable snapshot it dismisses or deletes approved/pending evidence whose exact head/base-branch identity differs from live and preserves evidence matching live. Evidence created after the snapshot is never touched. The handler then continues from the reloaded live identity. | **Why:** a delayed A-to-B event after A-to-B-to-A has the same departed ref as the final live base. Departed-ref filtering therefore cannot distinguish generations and can dismiss the fresh final-A approval, while simply ignoring the stale event can leave a numeric B approval active. Live-identity snapshot reconciliation handles A-to-B, A-to-B-to-C, and cyclic A-to-B-to-A delivery uniformly without letting a stale handler mutate later-created evidence. |
| D117 | **AMENDS D108-D116: GitHub review collection bodies are nullable input** (31 Jul 2026, operator + exact-head Codex/independent triage; roastpilot-agent#663/#679) | Every bridge scanner normalizes an explicitly present JSON `null` review body to empty text before marker filtering. String bodies remain unchanged. A missing `body` member or any non-null, non-string value remains a strict input error. Exact post-submit ownership validation still requires the deterministic bridge body and commit. | **Why:** GitHub permits ordinary approvals/comments without summary text and represents their body as `null`. Strict string parsing before identity filtering let one unrelated bodyless review strand approval publication, pending reconciliation, dismissal epochs, pagination, or retarget cleanup. Presence-sensitive nullable normalization follows the external API contract without weakening bridge-evidence matching or silently accepting malformed review objects. |
| D118 | **AMENDS D108: solo-maintainer privileged PRs use one explicit, audited default-branch operator override rather than an impossible self-review** (31 Jul 2026, operator + Codex engineer; roastpilot-agent#663) | Live activation preflight proved `syamaner` is the repository's only write-capable collaborator and GitHub forbids a PR author from approving their own PR. Once one approval is required, a workflow/helper PR authored by that maintainer would therefore deadlock. The trusted approval workflow accepts one typed `repository_dispatch` event, `privileged_review_override`, carrying a canonical positive PR number and a non-empty bounded reason. `repository_dispatch` is used instead of `workflow_dispatch` because GitHub executes it from the default branch; a ref-selectable manual workflow could run a PR branch's modified privileged code. The handler rechecks GitHub's current `role_name` for `maintain`/`admin` (with legacy `permission=admin` fallback; bare `write` is rejected), requires an open PR, and paginates its current file inventory for an affirmative edit to `.github/workflows/**` or the bridge helper. A complete ordinary inventory is ineligible; 30 full pages are indeterminate and block the override even when an earlier page contains a privileged match. The handler reloads and binds the exact PR/head/base identity through the existing two-phase approval path and publishes a distinct operator-override marker recording actor and URL-encoded reason, so audit text cannot inject bridge identity syntax. It never checks out PR code. Ordinary PRs cannot use the override. A repeated exact-identity dispatch is idempotent; a push or base retarget invalidates the approval like every other bridge review. Dependabot cleanup removes false normal/exemption evidence but preserves an exact-current operator override; retarget reconciliation still removes stale override identities. CI, Codecov, exact-head Codex, conversation resolution, and independent triage remain mandatory. | **Why:** D108 correctly rejected trusting a workflow-edit PR's Claude result, but its phrase "explicit maintainer approval" assumed a distinct non-author maintainer. The live repository has none, so the one-approval rule would turn every owner-authored workflow repair into a permanent protection deadlock. Temporarily lowering protection would be unaudited and weaken the very gate being restored. A maintainer-authenticated, reason-bearing default-branch dispatch preserves the explicit human decision and immutable PR identity while letting GitHub Actions supply the distinct counting reviewer identity required by the platform. `write` alone is deliberately insufficient for this exceptional power, an uninspectable maximum-size diff cannot self-qualify, and untrusted audit text cannot participate in identity matching. The dispatch remains exceptional and capability-scoped; it is not a general manual bypass for normal PRs. |
| D119 | **Bean-sourcing runtime monitoring records every admitted extraction attempt and correlates a successful draft to at most one explicit save through an opaque attempt id** (31 Jul 2026, operator + Codex engineer; roastpilot-agent#588) | Add one SQLite attempt ledger row after draft admission/idle validation and before fetch/provider work, so fetch failures, extraction failures, provider failures/timeouts, roast-start preemption, cancellation, success, and successful-but-never-saved drafts remain countable. The row records activated provider + resolved extraction model + controlled extraction prompt/schema version, start/completion timestamps, total latency, nullable retry-inclusive input/output token counts plus an `exact / partial / unknown` usage-evidence state, timeout count, outcome category, and successful draft provenance counts. Reported usage is retained when available; a timeout or transport failure with no provider usage response records unknown, never a misleading zero or invented estimate. Runtime does NOT freeze mutable provider prices into historical dollar cost: provider/model + the qualified token evidence are the durable spend facts and pricing remains an analysis policy. A successful draft returns a random opaque `draft_attempt_id`; add-mode SPA state carries it only into the existing create-profile request as metadata. Manual creates omit it. The store transaction that creates the bean profile also claims one still-unclaimed successful attempt and writes a canonical list of changed extraction-controlled profile field names; `source_url` and provenance-only fields are deliberately outside correction scoring because they are not model-extracted values; unknown, non-successful, expired, or already-claimed ids fail closed without creating a profile. The comparison uses the server-recorded normalized draft snapshot, never a client-supplied baseline; URL edits remain savable but are neither retained pre-save nor counted as extraction corrections. A successful attempt is claimable for 24 hours from completion. A service-owned periodic expiry task clears expired unclaimed snapshots on schedule while the process is idle; initialization and every later attempt/save also run the same idempotent expiry operation so restart and boundary races are covered. Expiry clears the normalized snapshot while retaining the opaque ledger id and aggregate attempt metrics; an arbitrarily old draft can never be attached. The ledger never stores the fetched HTML, prompt, raw provider response, error text, evidence quotes, credentials, or URL query/fragment. The normalized draft snapshot may contain the operator-visible profile values needed for equality comparison, but excludes `source_url`, provenance/evidence maps, and scouting prose; after save or expiry, only aggregate metrics, changed field names (on save), and the optional saved profile id remain. Cancellation finalization runs in a separately owned, shielded task and is boundedly awaited to commit the cancelled/preempted outcome before cancellation is re-raised; failure to persist that terminal state is surfaced, never silently reported as complete telemetry. No dashboard, alerts, corpus expansion, new bake-off, plausibility rules, or roast-control change ships in this slice. | **Why:** #588's extraction bake-off/corpus already shipped; the remaining product gap is real-world correction evidence. The existing API returns an unpersisted draft and later receives a generic create request, so there is currently no trustworthy server-side join between what the model proposed and what the operator saved. An opaque one-use id preserves the human save gate without contaminating `BeanProfileInput` or requiring raw URL retention. Attempt-first persistence makes still-billable failures visible instead of measuring only success. Qualified usage distinguishes measured spend evidence from calls whose provider response was lost, while dollar pricing remains mutable; storing error classes rather than messages avoids duplicating vendor/provider secrets. The scheduled 24-hour claim expiry bounds retention even during an idle process and preserves the normal review/save interaction. The end-to-end correlation is one coherent slice: shipping either the backend id or the SPA consumer alone would create dead telemetry. Security review is mandatory because this extends storage of untrusted extraction output; QA and UI review cover migration, lifecycle, cancellation-safe finalization, expiry, atomic one-use save, and client correlation. |
| D120 | **Within a running agent process, unconfirmed between-roast MCP teardown is cleared only by an explicit, audited, incident-bound hardware-clear acknowledgement** (1 Aug 2026, operator + safety/security/QA review; roastpilot-agent#668) | When an MCP lifecycle cannot confirm teardown, the process mints one opaque 128-bit incident id and exposes it read-only with health state. Within that running agent process, a fresh MCP owner is blocked even after the old owner finishes; `start()` never clears the verdict. Only `POST /api/mcp/acknowledge-hardware-clear` may consume that process-local incident, with literal `hardware_clear=true`, the exact current incident id, and a required trimmed bounded reason. Under the same roast-start lock, the service refuses every persisted or process-tracked active/recovery run, every attached MCP session, and every owner task that is still running or unwinding. A completed retained owner is permitted and drained as part of consuming the stale generation. The service rechecks the exact incident before committing the accepted audit row, commits that row before clearing state, then rechecks and consumes the incident. A lifecycle change during that audit await records a separate failed result and preserves the incident; it cannot make the accepted row count as a completed acknowledgement. Successful consumption clears stale generation/PID/config state and invalidates the spawn baseline. The action issues no MCP tool call and never resumes heat, fan, or cooling. A delayed acknowledgement for incident A cannot clear later incident B. The endpoint has a small request-body cap plus non-queuing monotonic admission before the roast-start lock; requests rejected before the accepted audit write, including stale/rate-limited probes, do not grow the durable audit table. The incident identity is not persisted across agent processes: physical verification plus a controlled full agent restart remains the legacy recovery boundary and produces no acknowledgement audit row. Durable cross-restart incident enforcement would require a separate persistence/startup-gating decision. | **Why:** restart-only recovery is operationally blunt and unaudited, so this decision adds a typed in-process alternative without silently redefining restart semantics. Binding the human decision to the incident the operator actually inspected preserves fail-closed lifecycle ownership while allowing in-process recovery after physical verification. Keeping start blocked at the low-level process boundary prevents alternate callers within that process from bypassing the service audit. This endpoint does not claim caller authentication: the current single-operator LAN API has no authentication boundary, and adding one remains the app-wide #595 decision rather than a hidden requirement of this slice. Rejected-probe persistence is deliberately omitted because that LAN API also has no app-wide rate-limit policy yet; otherwise a safety endpoint could amplify SQLite writes and contend with roast start. This decision does not weaken restart recovery: any possibly-active persisted run still enters `operator_recovery_required`, and the acknowledgement cannot bypass it. |
| D121 | **Catalogue recommendations use one bounded collection-page extraction plus deterministic local ranking; choosing a recommendation reuses the existing single-product draft and explicit-save flow** (1 Aug 2026, operator; roastpilot-agent#573) | `POST /api/beans/recommend-from-catalogue` accepts one operator-supplied public HTTP(S) collection URL through the existing bean-sourcing admission, SSRF-pinned fetch, decompression, parse, provider-timeout, cancellation, and roast-start-preemption boundaries. One bounded page yields at most 24 normalized, deduplicated same-origin product candidates from deterministic JSON-LD/anchor discovery; the server assigns opaque candidate ids and never trusts a model-generated URL. One typed provider call may extract bounded identity metadata for at most 12 discovered candidates from a clearly delimited data payload. The provider has no tools and cannot save a profile, order coffee, call MCP, or choose its own candidate URL. The server then ranks candidates deterministically and explainably: reward a country missing from the active local roster, a processing method missing from it, a novel country/process pair, and affinity with country/process pairs in completed non-excluded local roasts rated at least four stars; ties preserve source order. Return at most three recommendations with the server-owned product URL, normalized metadata, numeric score, and machine-generated reason codes/text. Do not send roast history, tasting prose, source URLs, or operator notes to the provider; only the collection page is model input, while roster/rating aggregates remain local scoring data. A recommendation creates no profile and no order. When the operator selects one, the SPA calls the existing `POST /api/beans/draft-from-url` for that product URL, preserving the established provenance gates, conservative target drafting, one-use correction telemetry, editable preview, and explicit `POST /api/bean-profiles` save. Catalogue attempts are separately identifiable in the attempt ledger, reach a terminal outcome, retain only aggregate usage/latency/counts, and never retain the collection URL, HTML, candidate URLs, provider output, recommendation text, or a claimable draft baseline. The first release is fetch-fresh and local-only: no saved vendor catalogue, notifications, cloud data, purchasing, auto-save, learned similarity, density/moisture schema, free-text flavour matching, or #567 revival. | **Why:** fetching and model-extracting every linked product would multiply spend, latency, and contention by catalogue size, while ranking from anchor labels alone would overclaim product knowledge. One bounded structured extraction makes cost independent of catalogue size after the fixed cap; deterministic ranking keeps the recommendation policy inspectable and testable. Local aggregate affinity uses existing ratings without exposing operator history to the model or pretending it is learned similarity. Reusing the hardened single-product flow after selection avoids duplicating evidence verification and avoids issuing multiple claimable draft baselines for recommendations the operator may never inspect. The story ships in two coherent PRs: backend contract/endpoint/telemetry first (security-reviewer + safety-reviewer + QA; a justified larger slice because discovery, candidate-id integrity, admission, terminalization, and ranking form one trust boundary), then the recommendation UI and story-close state update (QA + UI reviewer, plus cross-boundary contract tests). |
| D122 | **Joint drop objective: the advisor and taper must land target drop temperature and target DTR together, not first-past-the-post** (6 Aug 2026, operator; roastpilot-agent#707, evidence #559) | The 6 Aug paired Conebosque hardware A/B (baseline recovery-off 188 °C / 21% DTR; treatment recovery-on 190 °C / 24% DTR; both temp-short and DTR-over) falsifies the sufficiency of D89's development-window band (#499, closed): the advisor still drops on one target while missing the other, which is no better than a fixed Artisan if-then script. The programme is a four-workstream tree (#707), all defaults operator-ratified. (a) RP-A (#708), controller/safety: D96 recovery gains a runway-aware entry, a deterministic projected-drop-temp trigger firing when `bean + smoothed_ror × runway_to_dtr_max` lands more than `projection_margin_c` (default 3.0 °C, aligned to the drop-temp HIT tolerance) below target and never referencing the decaying setpoint, plus a one-time bounded fast-raise stepping heat to `entry + recovery_entry_step_pp` (default 10 pp) on entry; both preserve the D96 runaway-impossibility argument (earlier and faster to the same hard cap, non-latching projection) and require the Opus safety pass. (b) RP-B (#709), advisor-prompt: fan guidance conditioned on the #342 ambient triad, sanctioning aggressive fan at or above a starting threshold of ~26 °C and steering to graduated steps below it; prompt-only with no deterministic slew clamp in the first release; the threshold is a hypothesis to refit from data, as the current 11-roast ambient corpus shows no clean ambient-to-fan correlation. (c) RP-C (#710), a deterministic joint-window drop planner feeding the advisor projected (drop-temp, DTR) feasibility and a `window_status` signal; depends on #705 so the advisor is told a computed DTR rather than computing it. When both targets cannot be met the ratified priority is to hold for temperature and accept DTR overrun, bounded by the mandatory 196 °C ceiling guard. (d) RP-D (#711), the eval done first: a joint HIT metric (`|drop-temp error| ≤ 3 °C` and `DTR ∈ target ± 2 pp`) plus a 50/50-weighted scalar for ranking misses, wired into the replay bake-off and serving #396 and #705. All work is validated offline first; hardware validation is one variable at a time (a #559-style paired A/B), RP-B before RP-A, per operator ruling. `recovery_enabled` stays default-off until the joint objective is met on hardware and the cup is evaluated. | **Why:** an advisor that can satisfy only one of the two drop targets adds nothing over deterministic rules; the justification for an LLM in the loop is coordinating heat, fan and drop timing to arbitrate the trade-off when both cannot be met. Splitting deterministic computation (DTR, drop-window feasibility, recovery trigger and cap) from LLM judgement keeps the safety-critical parts inspectable and testable while reserving the model for the genuine trade-off. The metric ships first so every downstream change is judged against a fixed, operator-ratified definition of success rather than vibes. |
| D123 | **Catalogue discovery keeps `products.json` full membership for a query-scoped collection URL; do not honour storefront filter/sort scoping (roastpilot-agent#718 closed won't-fix)** (7 Aug 2026, operator; roastpilot-agent#718, follows #712/#715) | `_products_json_url` drops the collection URL's query string, so a filter/sort-scoped view (`?filter.v.availability=1`, `?sort_by=price`, `?q=`) is discovered as the collection's full membership rather than the operator's scoped view. The considered fix (detect scope params and fall back to HTML discovery for those URLs) is rejected: it reintroduces #712, since HTML anchor discovery is exactly the path that grabs site-wide cross-sell chrome and crowds the real green-coffee products out. Shopify's `products.json` is the collection's membership and does not honour filter/sort params, so forwarding them is a no-op. Benign tracking params (`utm_*`, `ref`) are already dropped and correctly do not affect discovery. Full membership stays. | **Why:** a storefront filter/sort is a presentation layer, not a different membership, and the recommendation feature (#712) is defined on membership; recommendations are then re-ranked deterministically (sort order irrelevant) and reviewed/edited by the operator before any save or purchase, so the only residual symptom is occasionally surfacing a filtered-out bean the operator simply does not pick. Trading a real #712 crowding regression for that cosmetic case is net-negative. The follow-up batch shipped alongside: #714 (scouting-note false-safety-claim copy), #716 (tags evidence sub-budget, shortest-first), #717 (locale-prefix product URLs), and #705 part 1 (selectable prompt c10 feeding the advisor the deterministic DTR; c3 stays default, c10 promotion gated on the #705 part-2 bake-off through #396). |
| D124 | **RP-D joint-objective bake-off scorer + RP-B c11 ambient-fan doctrine: design ratified for build** (7 Aug 2026, operator; roastpilot-agent#711, #709, under #707/D122) | RP-D scorer: a pure offline `joint_window_score(...)` core in `scripts/bakeoff_replay.py` (no LLM), applied by the standalone `scripts/rpd_corpus_score.py` corpus scorer, reporting the operator-ratified joint metric — binary HIT (`|drop_bean_temp − target_drop_temp_c| ≤ 3 °C` AND `DTR ∈ target ± 2 pp`, primary) and the scalar `max(0, 1 − (0.5·|Δtemp|/3 + 0.5·|ΔDTR|/2)/2) × termination_penalty` (secondary, ranks non-hits; the literal 50/50-plus-half form scores a window-edge roast 0.5 and a perfect roast 1.0, operator-confirmed), with `termination_penalty` = 1.0 for a normal drop and 0.0 for a guard-drop/emergency/faulted/aborted termination, and the D42 operator rating printed alongside every score (Goodhart guard). Inputs are read, never recomputed (the #705/c10 discipline): drop bean temp from the drop-event telemetry row (never peak, never the max-`development_percent` cooling-tail row, which misreads the Conebosque drops as ~142/168 °C), DTR from the deterministic #219/#220 `development_percent` clock, targets from the frozen `profile_json`. The corpus scorer reads each completed run's frozen `profile_json` targets, the deterministic `development_percent` clock, and the drop event, scoring every finished non-excluded roast; it is deliberately NOT a per-candidate `advisor_bakeoff.py` replay criterion, because RP-D scores a roast's achieved OUTCOME (a fixed property of the roast) rather than a candidate's tick-by-tick agreement, and the replay context's targets default to the achieved values (a self-comparison that would always HIT). Eval-only, no safety/controller/enum surface (qa review, not safety-reviewer). Corpus validation: both #559 Conebosque arms MISS and score 0.00 (baseline 188 °C/21%, treatment 190 °C/24%), and the store holds no joint-HIT yet. PR plan D1 scorer core / D2 bake-off wiring / D3 register both Conebosque fixtures. RP-B c11: `c11 = c10 + one _C11_AMBIENT_FAN_SECTION` spliced before THE OBJECTIVE (c1..c10 preserved byte-for-byte) qualifying c3's fan-brake by the #342 ambient triad — at/above ~26 °C aggressive fan (incl. 100) sanctioned, below it graduated steps (≤ ~15 pp) watching the RoR response with an explicit fan-slam-crashes-RoR warning; no threshold number is written into the prose (it comes from the `ambient_temp_c` context field, the #218 two-copies discipline), and #498's fan-100 capability is unchanged. `AdvisorContext` gains read-only `ambient_temp_c`/`ambient_humidity_pct` populated from `RoastTelemetry`'s live triad (#464/D86), no lever and no control read-back, carried to the model via the existing `model_dump_json`; `/config` gains a c11 selector option; c11 is selectable-only with c3 the live default; the deterministic fan slew clamp stays out of the first release (prompt-only, as ratified). Corpus caveat recorded: 10 ambient roasts (23.1–31.6 °C) show no clean ambient→fan pin (Brazil ran fan-100 at 23.9 °C and cupped a 4), so ~26 °C is a hypothesis to refit from RP-D scores and the below-threshold doctrine steers process, not a fan cap. Evaluated OFFLINE by decision-level fan-direction agreement at recorded ambient (RP-D CANNOT discriminate a fan-doctrine change in replay — it scores the fixed achieved outcome, identical across prompt arms; see D125), with RP-D reserved as the acceptance gate on the operator-gated single-variable HARDWARE roast. PR plan B1 doctrine (qa + safety-reviewer context-surface check) / B2 optional clamp deferred. | **Why:** D122 ratified the joint-objective targets and sequence; this records the design-level mechanism so the metric and the doctrine are built to a fixed, reviewed specification. The scorer ships first because RP-B's HARDWARE acceptance is defined as an RP-D score delta (the offline c3-vs-c11 comparison is decision-level, since replay cannot change the outcome RP-D measures — D125), and keeping the metric a pure trace function (no LLM, no safety surface) makes success measurable and cheap to re-run. c11 qualifies rather than replaces the live fan-brake teaching so the fix cannot regress #498's hot-ambient capability, and holding the threshold out of the prose lets it refit from data without a prompt edit. |
| D125 | **RP-D discriminates a control/prompt change only on hardware (or a validated plant simulator), not in replay; offline RP-B/RP-A comparison is decision-level** (8 Aug 2026, agent finding via Codex on roastpilot-agent#727; corrects the offline-eval premise of D122/#711/D124) | RP-D scores a roast's ACHIEVED outcome (drop temperature + DTR read from the trace). A replay re-runs a candidate advisor over FIXED recorded ticks and scores its DECISIONS for agreement; it does not re-simulate the plant, so the achieved drop temperature/DTR are unchanged across prompt arms. Therefore a c3-vs-c11 replay (and any advisor-prompt / fan-doctrine A/B) yields an IDENTICAL RP-D score for both arms offline — RP-D cannot measure the doctrine's effect in replay, and treating an offline RP-D delta as acceptance could falsely accept or reject c11. The offline comparison of a fan/heat doctrine (RP-B, RP-A) must use DECISION-level metrics (fan-direction / heat-direction agreement at the recorded ambient and state, drop-timing agreement), reserving RP-D as the acceptance gate on a real hardware roast (a #559-style single-variable A/B) where the doctrine actually changes the physical outcome — or on a validated plant simulator once the #580 plant-model track reaches GO. Nuance: RP-D COULD score a candidate's counterfactual DROP-TIMING offline by reading the recorded trace at the candidate's chosen drop tick (relevant to RP-C's drop planner), but that is not built and still cannot capture a fan doctrine's effect on the rate of rise, which needs a simulator or hardware. | **Why:** D122/#711/D124 framed RP-B as "measured by RP-D score deltas"; that holds only where the outcome is counterfactual (hardware or a validated simulator), not in replay, where the physical trace is fixed and both prompt arms inherit it. Recording the distinction prevents a false offline accept/reject of a prompt arm and keeps RP-D's role honest: it is the outcome yardstick for real roasts, while decision-level agreement is the offline signal. |
| D126 | **RP-B below-threshold fan step bound is 10 pp (one Hottop fan level), refining D124's "about 15 pp"; and the bound is carried as context DATA, not prompt prose** (8 Aug 2026, operator; roastpilot-agent#709, refines D124) | The Hottop fan is a 0-10 INTEGER scale: the driver quantises every fan command with `(value + 5) // 10` (`coffee_roaster_mcp.drivers._percent_to_hottop_fan_scale`, pinned v0.1.13). A 15 pp step is therefore not representable as a fixed number of physical levels — from fan 30 it lands on level 5 (a 20 pp physical move), from fan 35 on level 5 (10 pp). Telling the model 15 while the machine moves 10 or 20 is a told-vs-enforced gap in exactly the path the doctrine exists to protect. A 10 pp step is exactly one level from EVERY legal starting value, so the told bound equals the physical move; this is the same quantisation fact `post_fc_deadband_threshold_percent` (also 10) already encodes. Implemented as `ControllerConfig.ambient_fan_step_max_pp` (default 10.0, gt=0.0, le=100.0) surfaced read-only into `AdvisorContext`, alongside `ambient_fan_threshold_c` (default 26.0, gt=0.0, le=60.0) which carries D124's ratified ~26 °C boundary the same way. **Both numbers live in config, never in the c11 prose**, which stays digit-free under test: D124 said the threshold "comes from the `ambient_temp_c` context field", but that field is the MEASUREMENT, so a comparison boundary had to exist somewhere — as data it re-fits from RP-D scores with no prompt edit and no fresh bake-off (the #218 two-copies discipline), whereas in prose it would need both. The bound governs the ORDINARY approach only and bounds the STEP, never the destination (the fan ceiling and every fan value, including the ceiling, stay reachable — #498 unchanged); it is explicitly overridden when heat is at its floor and the bean is still climbing toward the drop ceiling, where c3's fan-brake rule takes precedence and the brake is raised in one decisive move without regard to the bound. Both fields are advisory: no clamp, gate, or slew enforces either, consistent with the ratified prompt-only-no-clamp posture. | **Why:** two findings from the pre-open review of PR-B1. The safety lens showed that in post-first-crack loop mode the deterministic loop owns heat and FAN IS THE ADVISOR'S LEVER while the safety gate applies no slew limit, so this prose is the only thing shaping fan pace and it shapes an ACTUATED value — an unbounded "graduated steps" therefore still permits the fan slam the doctrine exists to prevent. The Codex lens then showed the ratified 15 pp is not hardware-representable. Recording both keeps the plan honest about a ratified number that implementation proved wrong, and keeps the told value equal to the enforced physical move. |
| D127 | **RP-B's comparison baseline is c10, not c3 — the A/B arm must isolate the ambient section** (8 Aug 2026, operator; roastpilot-agent#709/#731, refines the eval plan of D122/D124/D125) | `c11 = c10 + one ambient-fan section`, so selecting c11 also activates the whole inherited splice chain c4..c10 — the drop-decisiveness, heat-floor, heat-recovery, DTR-pace, pace/bottom-edge/fan-coupling, reference-roast, and DTR-authority teachings, each of which is selectable-only and carries its own unmet promotion gate. A c3-vs-c11 comparison therefore measures that entire stack, not RP-B: a positive result could not be attributed to the ambient doctrine, and a negative one could be caused by an inherited teaching rather than by the doctrine. That is precisely the confound D122's "one variable at a time" ruling exists to prevent. **Both the offline decision-level comparison and the single-variable HARDWARE roast therefore run c10 (arm A) against c11 (arm B)**, so the inherited teachings are present in BOTH arms and cancel, leaving the ambient section as the only delta. Accepted consequence, recorded explicitly: this answers "does the ambient fan doctrine work?" and does NOT answer "should production switch from c3 to c11?" — the latter is a separate promotion question that would additionally require the inherited versions' own gates to be satisfied, and the hardware arm deliberately runs unpromoted teachings on real beans, which is acceptable only because both arms share them. | **Why:** found by the Codex lens on PR #731 (P1). D124 specified the c11 construction and the kickoff brief specified a c3-vs-c11 comparison; those two are individually reasonable and jointly produce a confounded experiment, which is the kind of gap that only shows up when the construction and the eval plan are read together. Recording the baseline as c10 keeps RP-B's result attributable, and keeps the D122 sequence (RP-B validated first, then RP-A) meaningful — an unattributable RP-B result would poison the RP-A step that follows it. |
| D129 | **RP-B's offline eval set spans BOTH sides of the ambient boundary, registered as anonymised NAMES only** (8 Aug 2026, operator; roastpilot-agent#709 PR-B3, refines D124's "register both Conebosque fixtures") | D124's PR plan ended with "D3 register both Conebosque fixtures". Investigation showed that pair cannot validate the doctrine: both #559 Conebosque roasts recorded ambient **below** the boundary (23.05 °C and 23.54 °C, 0.49 °C apart), so a c10-vs-c11 comparison over them exercises only the below-threshold graduated-step branch. That still measures something real — c10 carries no ambient teaching at all, so the graduated guidance is a genuine delta — but it cannot test the doctrine's CENTRAL claim, that it is CONDITIONAL on ambient, which is the half guarding #498's hot-room capability. The ratified eval set is therefore a **four-point spread** across the 11 ambient-captured roasts: ~23.5 °C (well below), ~25.7 °C (just below), ~26.5 °C (just above), ~31.6 °C (well above). The 25.7/26.5 pair is 0.81 °C apart and straddles the boundary directly, testing the conditional SWITCH; the ~31.6 °C roast tests whether the at-or-above branch genuinely sanctions aggressive fan, which a roast 0.48 °C above the boundary cannot show. **Registration commits NAMES, never data.** The mechanism mirrors the existing Artisan bake-off precedent exactly (`advisor_bakeoff.fixture_path_for`/`resolve_test_set`, `.artisan-fixtures/` gitignored): a committed tuple of anonymised names carrying ONLY each roast's ambient position relative to the boundary, plus a name→path resolver that raises rather than silently scoring a partial set, plus one `.gitignore` line. Zero telemetry, zero operator notes, zero bean identity, zero real run ids, zero dates are committed; fixtures are regenerated locally by `store_to_fixture.py` and the name→run-id mapping stays local and gitignored. Names must not encode the supplier (never `conebosque-*`) nor any outcome metric. One otherwise-eligible ambient roast is unusable for a non-ambient reason (the #582 `excluded` discard flag), and the two hottest roasts are from the same day, so they sample one hot-room condition twice rather than two independent ones. | **Why:** D124 named the fixture pair before the eval design existed, and the pair was captured for the #559 recovery-law A/B — same day, same room, one variable, which is exactly what makes it a poor ambient-contrast set. Registering it would have produced a fixture nobody trusts and a flat result discovered only after the harness ran, the failure mode kickoff PR-planning exists to prevent. Recording the name-only mechanism alongside the selection keeps the privacy invariant (AGENTS.md: never commit roast logs or personal data; the Artisan precedent's names are the committable artefact) from being re-litigated by the next implementer, who would otherwise reach for the obvious literal fixture name and leak the supplier. |
| D128 | **A stale ambient reading is declined before it reaches the fan doctrine, by a structural gate plus a 90 s age bound** (8 Aug 2026, agent-proposed and **OPERATOR-RATIFIED same day**, the 90 s value chosen over the 60 s alternative with the bound-not-measurement caveat explicitly on the table; roastpilot-agent#732, blocks promotion of #709/#731) | Two independent guards, deliberately different in kind. **(1) Structural, no clock:** `project_live_ambient` gates on `status.ambient_running` as well as `status == "ok"`, because the MCP's `AmbientSessionRuntime._stop_locked` drops its reader while LEAVING `status` at `"ok"` and PRESERVING the last reading, after which `poll`'s `self._reader is None` early return means that reading can never change again — a stopped runtime otherwise forwards one frozen room temperature forever, indistinguishable from a fresh one. **(2) Age bound:** `ControllerConfig.ambient_fan_doctrine.max_reading_age_seconds` (default **90.0**, `gt=0.0`, and bounded above — `le=600.0`, ten minutes, on the reasoning that a freshness bound longer than a roast cannot be a real one) declines a reading that is merely old while the runtime still runs. The upper bound is not decoration: it is the second occurrence of the defect class that independent triage flagged as the BLOCKER on PR #731, where `step_max_pp` shipped `le=100.0` against a stated intent of 10-20. A hand-refit knob whose ceiling sits far above its intent lets a plausible typo (`900.0` for `90.0`) validate cleanly and silently disable the guard for most of a roast, so every knob in this sub-model carries a ceiling near its intent plus a rejection test. 90 s is **three cycles** of the MCP's own `AmbientConfig.poll_interval_seconds` (default 30.0): below three cycles the doctrine flaps on ordinary poll jitter plus a couple of missed reads, well above it a wedged reading survives into the post-FC window where the doctrine acts. The age is measured ENTIRELY in the agent's clock, keyed on the FORWARDED reading; the MCP's `last_reading_monotonic_seconds` is used only as an opaque identity token compared for CHANGE, never subtracted, because cross-process monotonic clocks share no epoch (the same rule `RoastController._backdated_now` already observes). Keying on the forwarded rather than the raw reading matters: the raw-token form keeps ageing a reading nothing consumes and would then decline a RETURNING probe for the length of its outage. A declined reading degrades to the all-`None` absent-ambient triad, which c11's fallback already handles and which deliberately does NOT soften the fan (#498 direction preserved). Inert-consistent with #731: with `ambient_fan_doctrine.enabled=False` nothing about what reaches the advisor changes. **Recorded caveat: 90 s is a BOUND, not a MEASUREMENT.** Unlike the ~26 °C threshold there is no corpus behind it — nothing in the 10 labelled ambient roasts records reading age — so it is picked off the MCP's own poll cadence. The cost of it being wrong is asymmetric and mild in the safe direction: too tight merely falls back to the unqualified fan brake, too loose is the failure being fixed. 60 s (two cycles) is the defensible tighter option; below two cycles is not recommended. It is config, so a re-fit needs no code change and no fresh bake-off (the #218 two-copies discipline, as with D126's two numbers). | **Why:** #732 was raised because #709 makes ambient LOAD-BEARING for the first time. While ambient was observability-only (#342/D86) a frozen reading was a cosmetic dashboard defect; once c11 selects a fan regime on it, a stale LOW reading holds the model in the graduated regime after the room has warmed, which is exactly the #498 direction. The two guards are split by kind on purpose: the stopped-runtime case is a boolean fact needing no clock and no tuned number, so it is caught structurally and immediately, leaving the tuned bound responsible only for the genuinely-old-but-running case where a number is unavoidable. Recording the bound's provenance honestly keeps it re-fittable, and keeps it from later being mistaken for a corpus-derived value like the RP-D targets. |
| D151 | **The `gpt-4o` advisor pin (D43/#277) is a DEFAULT the operator may change, not a lock — `model_slug_by_phase` ships EMPTY, and the removed accidental protection is replaced by a pre-charge WARNING, not a hard guard** (9 Aug 2026, operator; roastpilot-agent#747, found while fixing #746) | **`AdvisorConfig.model_slug_by_phase` now defaults to `{}`**, so `model_for(phase)` falls back to `model_slug` in every phase and a model set in `/config`, in the saved config file, or via `ROASTPILOT_ADVISOR__MODEL_SLUG` is the model that answers a live roast. The #173 per-phase mechanism is retained (an override still wins) and stays out of `AdvisorConfigEdit`: under D35 only DEVELOPMENT consults the advisor, so the map has exactly one live slot and a per-phase UI would render three inputs for one. **Sub-decision — soft warning, not a hard guard.** The populated map had been providing real protection: D40/D41 measured `sonnet-4.6` / `gpt-5.5` / `opus-4.8` / `gpt-5-mini@low` busting the ~5 s post-FC latency slot, and until now an operator physically could not put one on a live roast. A runtime allow-list was REJECTED — it needs a new source of truth in `src/` (the only roster is `scripts/advisor_bakeoff.ROSTER`, whose `latency_risk` flag is a bake-off scoping hint), goes stale the day a model ships (`gpt-5.6-luna` would have needed a code change before it could be tried), and rejects at config-save time far from the roast. Instead a three-class note off the RESOLVED advice model is carried BOTH on the `#746` pre-charge banner AND on `GET /api/config` (the `/config` Model field), from ONE server-side source (`advisor_screen.py`) so the launcher and the UI cannot drift — the banner alone was a launch-time snapshot and therefore silent on the very path the A/B workflow uses, since `/config` applies next-roast under D78 (operator, 9 Aug, folding #754 into the same PR after three independent lenses flagged it). The classes: screened-and-clear is SILENT (so an ordinary roast stays quiet and the warning keeps its meaning), screened-and-BUSTED is named with its cost, and no-screen-on-record is named as unverified rather than as bad. Two advisory-only tables in `config.py` carry the classification, keyed by ARM `(endpoint, slug, reasoning_effort)` rather than by slug — keying on the slug alone INVERTS the record for `gpt-5.5 + reasoning_effort="off"`, measured at 2.9 s PASSING on 8 Jun while the same slug busts at the provider default; numbers stay in the reports so the code cannot drift into a stale citation. **`ControllerConfig.advisory_timeout_seconds` also drops 10.0 -> 5.0** (operator, same session): that value IS the bound on how long a slow model holds the control loop off its next safety evaluation and its next drain of the operator queue, so holding it at 2x the ~5 s screen let an unscreened model delay the loop for twice as long as any model we would knowingly run. | **This is a bug fix, not a re-pin — the pin's VALUE is untouched; what changed is that it is now the field default rather than an override the operator could neither see nor reach.** The map shipped populated with the pinned model for every phase and is absent from `AdvisorConfigEdit`, so it silently shadowed every operator-set `model_slug` for six weeks: `/config` rendered an editable Model box, `GET /api/config` reported the saved value `effective`, and the roast ran gpt-4o regardless. **Evidence gathered before deciding (#747 phase 1), from the trace DB: across all 36 runs only two models ever answered — `gemini-3.1-flash-lite` (14 Jun, the D33 pin) and `openai/gpt-4o` (every run from 21 Jun on). No live roast has EVER run a model other than the phase-map default.** **Roast 8 (`f3fc65fa`, 28 Jun, Colombia Excelso Huila) is the one affected roast: launched as a `gpt-4.1-mini` arm (PR #408's commit message, written 32 min after the roast; the launcher header it added; D73/D74; #396), it ran `openai/gpt-4o` for all 19 decisions.** The prompt arm (`c6`) DID switch — `prompt_version` was never shadowed. So **D74's headline is wrong** (its c6 hardware verdict was measured on gpt-4o, not mini) and **D73's caveat that gpt-4.1-mini had never run on hardware remains true today**. One accidental upside: roast 7 (gpt-4o + c3) vs roast 8 (gpt-4o + c6) is a CLEAN single-variable prompt comparison, a better experiment than the confounded model+prompt change it was recorded as. **Bake-offs are unaffected** — every `AdvisorConfig` construction in `advisor_bakeoff.py`, `bakeoff_reference_567.py` and `bakeoff_bean_sourcing.py` sets BOTH fields, and `bakeoff_replay.py` builds none, so every published result genuinely ran the model it names; `advisor_smoke.py` did read the base slug while calling the resolved one and is fixed in the same PR. **#396's offline method is sound; its HARDWARE-arm method was not** — D73's "score mini+c4/mini+c6 against gpt-4o+c1" and the 16 Jul "Luna earns a hardware A/B arm" both switch arms through the shadowed path, so a hardware model arm was impossible until this fix. **The load-bearing protection was never the model list and is unchanged:** `_maybe_run_advisory` is timeout-bounded and never blocks the tick, a failure becomes a REJECT with the deterministic hold-current-targets fallback, and D30's consecutive-failure stop arms on a sustained outage — a slow model degrades the ADVICE (late at the drop), it cannot stall the loop. That is why a warning is proportionate where a gate is not. **Residual, recorded not fixed:** the banner is a LAUNCH-TIME snapshot, so a model changed in `/config` between roasts gets no fresh warning (the #746 residual, unchanged here); and `roast_runs.config_json` freezes only `ControllerConfig` + `SafetyLimits`, so the REQUESTED advisor config is not in the trace at all — it was only provable after the fact because `advisor_decisions.model` records the phase-RESOLVED slug (#189). That gap narrows on its own now that requested and resolved coincide. Cross-refs: D43/#277 (the pin), D33 (the prior pin), D35 (only DEVELOPMENT consults), D40/D41 (the latency screen), D73/D74 (the roast-8 write-ups this corrects), D30 (the availability stop), #173 (the per-phase mechanism), #189 (`descriptor_for`), #396 (the A/B whose hardware method needs correcting), #746/#748 (the banner containment this fixes properly), #747. |
| D152 | **Codex-MCP becomes the DEFAULT implementer for specced slices in roastpilot-agent — including safety-critical ones — mirroring roastpilot-cloud's D145 credit pivot; effective immediately on the cloud repo's results, no local trial required** (9 Aug 2026, operator) | Implementation delegation defaults to the operator-level Codex MCP binding (`codex mcp-server` in `~/.claude.json`, deliberately outside the repo's review surface): the orchestrator delegates via `mcp__codex__codex` and folds review findings by continuing the same session with `mcp__codex__codex-reply`. **The load-bearing prerequisite is a written contract** — the operator's stated key factor is that Codex needs a very clear plan and specification — so a new read-only `story-planner` role (claude-fable-5, modelled on the cloud repo's) turns each story into an implementation contract (acceptance criteria, behavioural + negative test list, per-guard mutation checks, PR plan, reviewer routing) before any code, and delegation is **fail-closed: no contract, no Codex delegation**. `engineer-be` / `engineer-fe` become the FALLBACK implementers (Codex unavailable, or quota in reserve). Safety-critical slices (safety.py / controller.py / models.py enums / recovery paths) are INCLUDED in the default (operator choice over an exclude-safety option): the existing mandatory `safety-reviewer` (Opus, xhigh) floor is unchanged and becomes a genuine cross-family adversarial lens over Codex-authored safety code. Budget rule as in cloud: one weekly-capped Codex subscription shared across repos; both repos' every-PR pre-open review floors are protected first, implementation delegation flexes, and below ~20% remaining allowance implementation delegation stops entirely. On a Codex-authored branch the local `codex review` lens is same-family, so the Claude domain reviewers are the diverse lens there (pr-preflight step 5 wording updated). D23 unchanged: the author — Codex included — never adjudicates review feedback on its own PR. | The economics are the cloud repo's D145 ones and the operator confirmed the results there are good enough to adopt without a local trial: Anthropic credits are scarce, the Codex subscription comparatively plentiful, and Codex ships settled contracts efficiently while burning a full design round on ambiguity — so the contract bar is where the budget lever lives, and the spec-first discipline this repo already half-carries (PR-plan-at-kickoff, planning-architect) gets its missing piece in `story-planner`. Including safety-critical slices follows the cloud's proof cases (#80/#187: the cross-family Claude floor caught fail-opens a same-family author pass missed) — the protection is the mandatory Opus safety floor plus the gates and pin-guarded enums, not which family typed the diff. Delegation prompts must carry this repo's known traps explicitly: explicit worktree path with per-command self-location, the #738 fresh-venv-in-worktree rule, `.venv/bin/python -m ...` invocation, and full gates before handback. Cross-refs: cloud AGENTS.md Axis A / D145 (the pattern), D23 (independent triage), roastpilot-agent#761/#762 (the reviewer-recall work this composes with), tests/test_agent_model_pins.py (story-planner joins the pin map). |
| D153 | **The two standalone advisor-evaluation threads — the #396 prompt/model A/B and the #559 D96 recovery-law validation gate — are RETIRED into the #707 joint-drop-objective tree; neither is re-run as specified** (10 Aug 2026, lead; roastpilot-agent#396, #559) | **#396 (prompt + model A/B).** Its offline method was sound and its results stand as recorded; what is retired is the DECISION it was holding open. Two independent things moved under it. (a) Its hardware-arm method was never executable: D151 established that `model_slug_by_phase` shadowed `model_slug` in every advisor phase for six weeks, so roast 8 — the one roast recorded as a `gpt-4.1-mini` arm — actually ran `openai/gpt-4o` for all 19 decisions, and *every* proposed hardware model arm (D73's "score mini+c4/mini+c6 against gpt-4o+c1", the 16 Jul "Luna earns a hardware A/B arm") switched arms through the shadowed path. The model half of #396 therefore has no valid hardware evidence to weigh and never could have produced any before D151's fix. (b) The prompt half has moved: the live prompt question is now c10 (the deterministic-DTR teaching, #705 part 1) versus c11 (the ambient fan doctrine, #709/RP-B), evaluated by the D127 comparison harness (#749) against the D129 eval set and gated for acceptance by the D124 joint-window metric (#711/RP-D) on a single-variable hardware roast — not by c1-vs-c3 drop-F1 on the 17-roast Artisan set. **#559 (D96 recovery law).** The paired Guatemala Conebosque hardware validation this issue existed to gate RAN on 6 Aug 2026 on `main` at `e3c67f0`, both arms in the trace DB: structural/safety PASS (the raise path genuinely exercised, `holding`→`recovering`, heat 60→67 inside the entry+15pp=75 cap, 196 °C guard armed, every write ALLOW, zero clamp/reject/fault), efficacy INSUFFICIENT on that bean (baseline 188 °C/21% DTR, treatment 190 °C/24%; both temp-short and DTR-over). That result is the founding evidence of the #707 tree (D122), so the gate has been satisfied and its findings inherited rather than left pending. **`recovery_enabled` remains default-off and the cup remains the final gate** — that is unchanged by this close and is now carried by #708 (RP-A recovery v2: runway-aware trigger + fast-raise), which owns the diagnosed causes (recovery fired too late, at the DTR target with no runway because the decaying measured-anchored setpoint chased the crashing RoR down, and too weakly, 67 of an available 75). The under-expansion observation (first crack heard to peter out, ~75% jar fill both arms) is the RoR-crash fingerprint and points at #709's fan doctrine, not at the recovery law. | Both issues had become board state that outlived the question they encoded, which is the specific failure the `prevented-pre-pr` accounting exists to catch: leaving them open reads as "two evaluations still owed" when one is unrunnable as specified and the other has already produced its evidence. Retiring them is a shift-left win, not a scope cut — no work is dropped, it is re-homed onto issues that carry the current framing, and #396's retirement additionally prevents a *paid* OpenRouter bake-off from being run against a premise D151 falsified. The alternative (rewrite both issues in place) was rejected: #396's title, body and eight comments all encode the c1/c3/c4/c5/c6-versus-gpt-4.1-mini frame, and an issue whose every artifact contradicts its current scope is harder to read than a closed issue with a forward pointer. Cross-refs: D151 (the shadowing fix that invalidated the hardware-arm method), D43/D69/D73/D74 (the pin and the roast-8 write-ups #396 carried), D96 (the recovery law #559 gated), D122 (the joint-objective tree that absorbs both), D124/D125 (the RP-D metric and its hardware-only discrimination), D127 (the c10-vs-c11 comparison), roastpilot-agent#707/#708/#709/#711/#749. |
| D154 | **A normative control routed into more than one agent prompt is guarded by EXACT canonical-block match with one authoritative copy, never by keyword or proximity heuristics; and when successive review rounds each find a STRUCTURALLY DIFFERENT bypass rather than another instance, that is the signal to change the design's failure direction rather than patch again.** (10 Aug 2026, from #733 / PR #774) | The #733 guard began as an anchor-substring check over the eleven role prompts. QA round 1 demonstrated a false PASS: a discipline section rewritten to say the forbidden git commands "are all fine to use whenever convenient" kept every required anchor and stayed green — a guard that CERTIFIES a control which is not in force, which is worse than no guard. A negation-cue fix was then defeated four more ways in round 2 ("not forbidden" satisfies the cue with the word *forbidden*; a contradiction split across two bullets; "Never" scoped to one command while crediting five; a numbered bullet the chunker grouped differently). The redesign asserts each Bash-capable role carries its canonical block byte-for-byte, with the variant chosen from frontmatter write capability, from a directory-derived roster so a new role cannot slip through. The deliberate trade is that a faithful rewording FAILS: a routed control is not per-role prose, so it changes once at the authoritative copy and propagates in the same commit. A second guard applies the same logic to invocation sites, deriving the role-backed `agent()` calls in `review-branch.mjs` by scanning the file rather than listing known ones. | **Two sub-decisions recorded here rather than as their own numbers.** (1) NO separate D-number for the reviewer-worktree-at-the-reviewed-sha rule: `docs/agent-topology.md` §8 item 6 already makes it normative, and a plan-repo copy would create a second authoritative-looking statement of an operational control — the duplicate-authority failure this repo has already paid for. (2) The stopping rule must be declared BEFORE round one and honoured: rounds continue while any round produces a P1, and the first round with none ends the chain with surviving P2s filed to tracked issues. #733 ran six rounds; rounds 4 and 5 each fixed a defect introduced by the previous fix, and round 6 produced three P2s and no P1, so it stopped — deferrals #777, #778, #780. The generalisable warning is that a fix applied to an INSTANCE rather than to its CLASS reappears: patching `reviewBase` covered one of the workflow's `agent()` call sites and left the triage stage and the `triage-pr` skill broken, which is why the class-coverage guard scans rather than enumerates. Cross-refs: D152 (Codex-MCP as default implementer — this slice was its second delegation), D23 (independent triage; the author never adjudicated its own review), #733/#772/#774, #777/#778/#780, `docs/agent-topology.md` §7/§8/§16, `docs/agent-team-worktrees.md`. |
| D155 | **RP-B hardware acceptance FAILED: c11 binds but does not help, so it is NOT promoted and c3 remains the live default; the post-FC lever authority asymmetry (#781) is sequenced BEFORE RP-A's timing work (#708), which is re-scoped alongside it rather than replaced** (10 Aug 2026, operator; roastpilot-agent#709, #781, #708, #779, #711; closes the acceptance gate D124/D127 opened, under #707/D122) | The single-variable c10-vs-c11 hardware A/B that D127 specified RAN on 10 Aug 2026 (Guatemala Conebosque washed, 195 °C / 16 % targets, back-to-back, identical config apart from the prompt version): baseline `eaafde88` (c10) FC 179 / drop 183 / DTR 16.27 % / weight loss 10.4 %, treatment `0dcb58ec` (c11) FC 184 / drop 188 / DTR 16.44 % / weight loss 12.3 %. **`rpd_corpus_score`: HIT 0/2.** **The doctrine BINDS:** every fan step after the first was exactly `ambient_fan_step_max_pp` (D126's 10 pp), a signature the baseline never shows, so c11 demonstrably took effect and the D124/D126 mechanism works as specified. **The doctrine does NOT HELP:** by its own explicit wording it bounds the STEP and not the DESTINATION, so the advisor walked fan to **90** in disciplined increments — *higher* than the baseline's 65 — and the rate of rise collapsed 6.0 → 3.0 anyway. **The finding that decides it:** both arms gained **exactly +4 °C across development**, so the treatment's 5 °C better drop is INHERITED from its higher first crack (a warm-machine/run-order effect, confounded because the treatment was always second) rather than produced by the doctrine. **c11 is therefore not promoted, c3 stays the live default, and #709 stays open and inert.** The cup is not yet a gate input (both roasts unrated on the night; the structural verdict does not depend on tasting). **Consequential sequencing (operator, same night): #781 opens BEFORE #708's timing work.** #781 records that the two post-first-crack levers have wildly asymmetric authority — the advisor's fan is 0-100 with no slew limit, while the controller's heat is capped at `heat_engage + recovery_headroom` over a baseline pinned by the pre-FC trim (trim 60 capped heat authority at 60 for the whole development phase, 75 even with recovery fully active), so on the night the advisor moved its lever 60 pp against the controller's 10. #708 is **not demoted and its scope is unchanged** — the runway-aware trigger and bounded fast-raise both stay — and it inherits #781's bound as a precondition; the reason for the order is experimental, in that a timing A/B run before fan authority is bounded reproduces this night's exact confound and returns the same uninterpretable result. **#710 (RP-C) remains independently buildable**, needing no new control authority. **Two corrections that must travel with this result.** (1) **#708's premise was NOT falsified.** Recovery did enter with 4.9 pp of runway, meeting #708's own stated acceptance bar, and the outcome did not move — but the treatment ran early recovery AND c11 AND fan 90 while the baseline ran c10 and stopped at 65, so early entry was only ever tested UNDER MATERIALLY HIGHER AIRFLOW. The establishable claim is the narrow one: 4.9 pp was insufficient **at that airflow**. It cannot show entry timing is non-binding, and the stronger wording is dangerous precisely because it would license dropping timing work from a v2 scope. (2) **Two traps in the same dataset.** The baseline's 0.00 RP-D scalar IS the metric's genuine verdict on its numbers, and must NOT be attributed to the accidental post-drop e-stop: `joint_window_score` computes `scalar = max(0.0, raw) * penalty`, so the clamp runs BEFORE the termination penalty, and the 12 °C drop miss alone drives `raw` to −0.034, which floors at 0.00 regardless (recomputed from source: the baseline scores 0.0000 with `terminated_abnormally` either True or False; what the abnormal termination WOULD suppress is the HIT flag, already False on these numbers); and the quoted FC figures are measured at the CONFIRMATION instant, the very anchor #779 identifies as wrong (true onset is 1-2 °C lower), though the +4 °C development symmetry the verdict rests on survives either anchor. **Ambient gate restated:** the standing "room ≤ 24.0 °C at roast-1 charge" would have blocked a correct decision on a rounding margin (roast 1 charged at 24.08 and proceeding was right); the binding constraint is the SECOND roast, so the gate is now **evening slot; roast 2 charges below 26.0 °C; ≤ 24.5 °C at roast 1 as the proxy**, safe because evening pairs FALL rather than rise (4 of 5 in the corpus). | **Why:** D124 and D127 built RP-B to a fixed specification and defined its acceptance as an RP-D result on a single-variable hardware roast (D125 having established that replay cannot discriminate a fan doctrine at all). That gate has now been run and returned a negative, and recording the negative is what keeps the programme honest: the mechanism is confirmed working, so this is not a build defect to fix but a DOCTRINE that was aimed at the wrong quantity — bounding the pace of a lever cannot help when the lever's destination is what crashes the rate of rise, which is a structural fact about authority rather than anything a prompt can teach. Recording the #781-before-#708 order in the plan (and not only on the issues) matters because the tempting reading of "recovery met its bar and nothing moved" is that timing is a dead end, which would quietly delete RP-A's remaining scope on the strength of a confounded arm; stating the narrower establishable claim alongside the sequencing keeps #708 alive with its scope intact while still explaining why it waits. The two data traps are recorded because both were misread once already during the same night's write-up (the 0.00 twice, in successive corrections), and a number that reads plausibly is exactly the kind that survives into a later decision uncorrected. Cross-refs: D122 (the joint-objective tree), D124 (the c11 construction and RP-D metric), D125 (hardware-only discrimination), D126 (the 10 pp step bound this arm exercised), D127 (the c10 baseline that made the arm attributable), D129 (the offline eval set #749 still owes), D96/D153 (the recovery law whose second hardware firing this was), roastpilot-agent#709/#781/#708/#710/#779/#742/#726. |
| D156 | **The 6 Aug prompt-only ratification is SUPERSEDED: post-first-crack fan authority gains a deterministic, ambient-conditioned DESTINATION ceiling (value 70, flag-gated default OFF), and the trim-pins-post-FC-heat-authority sub-finding is split out docs-first** (11 Aug 2026, operator; roastpilot-agent#781, #709, #708, #710, #498, #707) | D124/D126 shipped the ambient fan doctrine as PROMPT-ONLY with a deterministic slew clamp explicitly not shipped, and the agent codified that ratification in a test docstring (`tests/test_controller.py:5107`, `test_ambient_fan_doctrine_is_advisory_only_and_actuates_no_lever`: "no fan slew clamp ships with this release (6 Aug ratification: prompt-only, no clamp)"). D155's hardware arm then showed the doctrine BINDS but does not HELP. This decision replaces the prompt-only posture with enforcement, and it does NOT adopt the shape the issue proposed. **Rejected: the slew clamp of the original menu**, because it bounds the STEP and not the DESTINATION, which is exactly what c11 already did: every step complied with the 10 pp bound and the advisor still reached fan 90, and since the taper applies the held desired fan at roughly a 5 s cadence, a per-write step bound merely spreads 30 -> 90 over about 30 s with the SAME endpoint. **Rejected: coupling the fan ceiling to remaining heat authority**, on two mechanical grounds -- the advisor consult and the taper write each resolve bounds through their own `_control_limits()` call, the write re-resolving a FRESH box, so a ceiling varying per tick with recovery entry/exit/glide would let the TOLD value and the ENFORCED value diverge between the two evaluations and break the told-equals-enforced-by-identity pattern (D-series #273/#294); and it would propagate the trim-pinned heat number into a second lever, entangling the sub-finding below. **ADOPTED: narrow `fan_ceiling_percent` on the DEVELOPMENT control box inside `limits_for`, so the EXISTING `command_bounds` CLAMP does the work.** `safety.py` does not change, no new `SafetyVerdict` is introduced, and runaway-impossibility is untouched because the ceiling only ever narrows and never raises a floor, while the FAULT/EMERGENCY paths set `adjusted_fan` directly without reading the box. Because both evaluation sites resolve the same `limits_for`, narrowing there updates what the model is TOLD and what is ENFORCED in one instance -- told equals enforced by structural identity rather than by prose. **Predicate:** the ceiling applies only when ambient is present, fresh and BELOW the doctrine threshold; absent, stale or non-finite ambient means NO clamp at all (fan free), failing toward preserving the #498 brake; and the #498 precedence carve-out that c11 carries in prose becomes deterministic -- when heat is at (or below) its post-FC EFFECTIVE FLOOR and the bean is still climbing, fan is the only brake left and is never rationed. **[Corrected in place, 11 Aug 2026: this row as first written said "at or below its post-FC base ceiling", which was a drifted paraphrase of the briefing, not the ratified predicate. Both authoritative sources -- c11's shipped prose at `config.py:1195-1199` and roastpilot-agent#781's own risk paragraph -- say FLOOR, and the operator ruled that the prose wins. The two are not close: under D88 the loop tapers within `[effective_floor, effective_ceiling]`, so `heat <= ceiling` holds on essentially every tick while heat may still hold 35+ pp of downward brake authority, so the superseded form would have released the fan ceiling exactly when heat could still brake. See D157.]** **Value 70**, because the Hottop fan is a 0-10 integer scale quantised `(value + 5) // 10` (D126), so 70 is physical level 7 -- precisely the level the c10 baseline arm's fan 65 actually actuated; it is a first-cut hypothesis to be re-fit from RP-D joint scores exactly like `threshold_c`. **The limits are ratified WITH the decision:** c10 stalled at that very airflow and was the WORSE arm (|dDrop| 12), so the guaranteed deliverable is closing the told-versus-enforced gap and holding airflow constant so #708's timing A/B becomes a single-variable experiment -- NOT fixing temp-short drops, which remains #710's job. Two slices, `safety-reviewer` mandatory on both: config + policy math, then controller wiring plus a CATEGORICAL truth-sweep of every copy still claiming no clamp ships or that the doctrine bounds the step and not the destination. **Sub-finding split out docs-first:** `heat_engage_percent` is inherited from the pre-first-crack trim (`controller.py:1418-1419` feeding `post_fc_control.py:440`), which then sets both the D88 base ceiling and the D96 recovery ceiling, so trim 60 silently caps post-FC heat at 60 and at 75 even with recovery fully active. Nothing in the trim docstring, `/config` or the launch banner says so, and THAT SILENCE is the reportable defect; the authority expansion itself waits until #708's timing A/B, run at bounded airflow, shows the heat side needs room. | **Why:** D155 established that the doctrine was aimed at the wrong quantity, and the natural next move -- ship the clamp that D124/D126 held back -- would have repeated the same error, because the held-back clamp was also a step bound. Recording the rejection of the literal menu option matters more than recording the adoption: the menu is what a later reader would otherwise pick up, and both of its deterministic options are the ones the code argues against. The decision is placed in the plan rather than only on the issue because it REVERSES a recorded operator ratification that is codified in the test suite, so a future reader finding that docstring must be able to trace why it changed; changing that test is the visible act of supersession, not incidental tidying. The ratified limits are recorded in the same breath as the decision for the reason D155 already had to correct twice in one night: a claim that reads plausibly survives into later decisions uncorrected, and "we bounded fan authority" would otherwise be inherited as "we fixed the temp-short drop". Flag-gated default OFF keeps the shipped default behaviour identical until a hardware arm earns the change, consistent with how D88's taper and the c9/c11 context groups were each introduced. The sub-finding is split rather than folded because it is the only candidate that would EXPAND authority upward (relaxing D88's never-add-heat-beyond-entry law), and it is untestable until fan is bounded -- the same sequencing argument D155 used for #781-before-#708, applied one level down. Cross-refs: D88 (the taper and its entry clamp), D96/D153 (the recovery law and its headroom), D122 (the joint-objective tree), D124/D126/D127 (the doctrine, its 10 pp step bound and the c10 baseline), D129 (the offline eval set #749 still owes), D154 (categorical guarding, which the slice-2 truth-sweep follows), D155 (the hardware verdict this answers), roastpilot-agent#781/#709/#708/#710/#498/#707. |
| D157 | **The post-first-crack fan destination ceiling is released by a ONE-WAY LATCH, and it is enforced at the ADVISOR CONSULT ONLY -- not at the taper write, and never inside `_control_limits()`** (11 Aug 2026, operator decision on the damping; lead resolution on the site; roastpilot-agent#781 slice 2) | **The oscillation hazard D156 did not foresee.** With heat pinned at its effective floor, fan is the sole input to the sign of rate-of-rise, and the sign of RoR gates fan's own ceiling -- a loop.
