Measured on the slice-1 branch in a cool room with heat at floor 25, RoR `+0.1` gave ceiling 100 while RoR `0.0` gave ceiling 70, so wiring it undamped would flip the box tick to tick AND tell the advisor a ceiling oscillating 70/100 with no explanation it can act on: the told-instability shape #563 burned on. **ADOPTED: the one-way latch** -- once heat first reaches its effective floor with the bean still climbing, the ceiling is released for the remainder of the dwell and nothing reads RoR again for that purpose. **Rejected: an RoR margin/deadband**, because it would add a THIRD unfitted first-cut constant beside `threshold_c` and the value 70, none of which has been fitted yet. The accepted cost, ratified with the decision: release is permanent, so a brief early dip to the floor disables the ceiling for the rest of that roast -- which is the fail-toward-no-clamp direction the whole predicate is built on, so the cost lands on the safe side. **Consequence for implementers: the conjunctive phrasing recorded on slice 1 ("heat at floor AND bean possibly climbing") describes the LATCHING CONDITION, not a per-tick gate.** **The enforcement site is corrected from D156.** D156 records that "both evaluation sites resolve the same `limits_for`" and infers that narrowing there covers consult and write together. The mechanism is not that: in loop mode the consult never writes -- it bounds-checks the advisor's request against the narrowed box and holds the CLAMPED result in `_post_fc_desired_fan_percent`, and the taper's coalesced write then actuates that already-clamped VALUE (`controller.py:2050-2062` says so in its own comment). Narrowing the write too would re-clamp a value that already passed its own bounds check, and narrowing the fail-safe-down raise-undo corrective at `controller.py:2424` -- which passes `requested_fan=self._current_fan` -- would turn a HEAT correction into a silent fan cut decided by no actor, the `safety-561` trap that site already documents for the heat lever. So the signal is passed at the consult alone; told equals enforced by instance identity there, and every other box resolution keeps the full 0-100 fan range. Accepted, documented residual: a desired fan set under a warm-room consult is still actuated by the taper if the room turns cool before the next consult; the window is bounded by the advisory cadence and its direction is free fan, hence #498-safe. | **Why:** the latch is recorded in the plan rather than only on the issue because it is an operator choice between two designs with different give-aways -- the latch surrenders the ceiling permanently after one qualifying tick, the margin keeps it live all run at the price of a fitted constant -- and a later reader finding a ceiling that stopped binding mid-roast must be able to tell the ratified behaviour from a bug. The site correction is recorded because D156's own mechanism sentence is what a future implementer would otherwise follow, and following it would produce either a no-actor fan cut or a told-versus-enforced divergence -- the exact failure D156 rejected the coupling design for. Recording it as a new decision rather than a silent rewrite of D156 keeps the supersession traceable, the same discipline D156 applied to the 6 Aug ratification. **[AMENDED 11 Aug 2026, by the operator, BEFORE any code shipped under this row -- recorded as an addendum rather than a silent rewrite.]** A pre-open adversarial safety review proved BY EXECUTION that the latch as first specified does NOT deliver the monotonicity it was chosen for: it damps only the RoR axis, so the ceiling the advisor is TOLD can still flip 70 -> 100 -> 70 within one dwell when the ambient probe goes stale (releasing the ceiling) and then reports fresh again (re-engaging it). Same told-instability shape, different mechanism. **Two amendments follow.** (1) **The latch extends to EVERY axis, stated as an invariant rather than as a list of conjuncts:** within a single DEVELOPMENT dwell the told fan ceiling is MONOTONE -- once the ceiling has resolved as engaged and subsequently resolves as not-engaged for any reason (stale, absent or non-finite ambient; a warm reading; the RoR carve-out), it never re-engages for the rest of that dwell. Stating it as an invariant rather than enumerating the axes is deliberate: the first formulation was an enumeration and the enumeration was incomplete, which is the D154 lesson applied to a control predicate instead of to prose. The direction is unchanged -- it only ever moves toward MORE fan authority, never less, so #498 is preserved. (2) **The c11 cool-room prompt bullet is reworded**, because it asserted "the fan ceiling is unchanged and every fan value stays available to you, including the ceiling itself" in precisely the branch the ceiling clamps -- a told-versus-enforced contradiction shipped inside the slice whose purpose is closing told-versus-enforced gaps. It now reads "every fan value up to the fan ceiling given in context", the idiom the WARM branch of the same prompt already used. The rewording is semantically null while the flags are OFF (the context ceiling is then 100) and truthful when they are ON, and it stays digit-free so the existing guard test holds; the operator accepted that c11's literal bytes change relative to the 10 Aug hardware arm. **Also recorded, because it changes how the #708 A/B must be configured:** enforcement is in practice a LOOP-MODE feature. With the post-FC control loop inert the effective heat floor is unknown (`None`), which makes the at-floor conjunct unconditionally true, so a climbing bean arms the release on the first DEVELOPMENT tick and the ceiling never binds. Enabling the two doctrine flags WITHOUT `post_first_crack_control.enabled` therefore holds no airflow constant and the A/B would silently measure nothing. **[AMENDED AGAIN 11 Aug 2026, by the operator, once more BEFORE the code shipped under this row -- recorded as a further addendum rather than a silent rewrite of the first one.]** **The release latch is RUN-scoped, not per-DEVELOPMENT-dwell.** Amendment (1) above states the monotonicity invariant over "a single DEVELOPMENT dwell"; the ratified scope is the whole RUN. Once the ceiling has resolved as engaged and subsequently resolves as not-engaged for any reason, it never re-engages for the remainder of the run, INCLUDING across an operator resume that re-enters DEVELOPMENT. **Why the scope changed:** the per-dwell form was implemented first and justified on consistency with the sibling per-engagement fields cleared in the same `transition_to` cluster (`_post_fc_engaged`, `_post_fc_desired_fan_percent`, `_last_post_fc_output`, `_post_fc_raise_suppressed_after_clamp`). That is a code-symmetry argument and it undersold the safety axis: per-dwell means a resumed run re-enters DEVELOPMENT UNLATCHED, so the ceiling can bind again on a roast where fan had already been established as the only remaining brake. **[Path corrected 11 Aug 2026, by execution, before merge -- the substance of this justification stands but its first statement named the wrong edge, and the wrong edge is the one a reader would check.]** The DIRECT `operator_recovery_required -> development` resume does NOT re-narrow under either scope: `transition_to` clears `_post_fc_engaged` unconditionally and only the TRUE first-crack edge sets it, the post-FC loop is gated on that flag, so `_last_post_fc_output` stays `None` and the predicate is declined by its unrelated floor-unknown carve-out before it ever reads the latch. The load-bearing path is one edge further out: `development -> operator_recovery_required -> ROASTING_PRE_FIRST_CRACK -> development`, which the transition table permits and `api.py`'s `_parse_resume_target` accepts, and whose second first-crack detection IS the true FC edge, so the loop re-engages. Measured on that path with `post_first_crack_control.enabled` true: the second dwell reports `_post_fc_engaged` true, a populated `_last_post_fc_output`, and a signal carrying `post_fc_heat_floor_percent = 25` -- a KNOWN floor, so the ceiling genuinely can bind there, and per-dwell scoping would have cleared the latch on the way. Recorded because an intermediate analysis concluded from the direct-resume edge alone that the run-scoping was behaviourally INERT, and that conclusion is wrong; it generalised from one edge to every re-entry, which is the D154 fix-the-instance-miss-the-class failure appearing in analysis rather than in a fix. A first probe of it also read as a confident negative purely because the baseline test config ships `post_first_crack_control.enabled=False`, so the loop was inert in BOTH dwells and the measurement verified nothing. Run-scoped is strictly more fan authority in exactly the state #498 exists to protect, and the direction rule this whole predicate is built on -- only ever toward MORE fan authority, never less -- decides it. **Mechanically:** the clear moves out of `transition_to` into the run-start path (`Controller.start_run`), and the field carries a comment at both sites saying why it deliberately does NOT clear with its per-dwell neighbours, because that symmetry argument is precisely what a later reader would use to "fix" it back. The arming and logging siblings (`_post_fc_fan_ceiling_engaged_once` and the two once-per-dwell log flags) stay per-dwell: once the run-scoped latch has released, nothing can engage or release again in that run, so their scope is unobservable from there on. **Neither scope survives an agent restart** -- the latch is in-memory and a restart builds a fresh controller -- and that is unchanged and acceptable, because a restart with a possibly-active run enters `operator_recovery_required` and never auto-resumes heat or fan. **One further correction ships in the same slice and is recorded here because it narrows the ratified PREDICATE, not merely its logging:** `post_fc_fan_ceiling_percent` accepts 100 (`ge=10, le=100`), and at that value the DEVELOPMENT box is not narrowed at all, so an "engagement" there is indistinguishable from the feature being off -- yet the controller logged an engagement and thereby licensed a matching release, which is the false-scoring-telemetry class whose four earlier members this slice already closed. `post_fc_fan_ceiling_engaged` now additionally requires the configured ceiling to be BELOW the unrestricted lever maximum, stated as the invariant "engagement means the box is narrowed" and placed in the predicate rather than added as another conjunct at the logging site -- a fifth member appeared at all because each of the previous four was fixed at its consuming site instead of at the predicate they share. The fail-direction is unchanged: with the predicate false the box resolves to the full 0-100 range it already had, and the release path can still arm, which moves toward MORE fan authority. Whether the config should instead REJECT 100 outright -- the delete-the-surface option -- is left open as an operator call; the predicate fix holds either way and does not depend on a validator that direct construction could bypass. Cross-refs: D156 (the ceiling this damps and the row whose carve-out phrasing was corrected in place), D88 (the taper and the `[effective_floor, effective_ceiling]` box the floor comes from), D96/D153 (recovery headroom), D124/D126 (the doctrine and its step bound), D154 (categorical guarding, which slice 2's truth-sweep follows), #563 (the told-instability precedent), roastpilot-agent#781/#498/#708/#710. |
| D158 | **Pilot a minimal Codex-led delivery topology: the top-level Codex session orchestrates delivery, while Codex and Claude form a reserve-aware implementation pool and Claude remains the independent planning and assurance family.** (12 Aug 2026, operator; supersedes D152's Claude-main-loop and fixed-local-review allocation for this pilot) | Human product, scope, architecture, hardware-safety, irreversible, and material cost authority is unchanged. Every delegated slice still requires a maintainer-ratified `story-planner` contract, fresh worktree and ignored-file checks, plan/base SHA binding, untrusted-input controls, and author-independent triage. Only the Codex parent may cross the family boundary; Claude and Codex workers are leaves, do not invoke the other family or spawn workers, reviewers do not edit, and repair receives lead-authored adjudicated directives rather than raw findings. At each slice boundary the parent reserves mandatory review/repair capacity, selects the capable family with healthier observable non-reserved capacity, asks for `healthy` / `constrained` / `reserve-only` when capacity is not observable, and never switches mid-slice merely for quota. Additional local review is minimum-sufficient and risk-routed: one independent diff-focused review ordinarily; mandatory safety and security triggers remain, with UI/MCP/simulation/QA/product-audit/triage roles invoked only on their stated capability or lifecycle triggers. GitHub exact-head Claude approval, ready-head Codex review, conversation resolution, CI, CodeQL handling, Codecov, and branch protection are unchanged. Implementation is the smallest repository-authoritative pilot: `AGENTS.md`, project-scoped `.codex/config.toml` plus three bounded Codex worker definitions, and only focused guard/skill updates needed for consistency; `docs/agent-topology.md` remains historical rather than being duplicated. |
| D159 | **The #811 `capture-agent-usage run` command is measurement/validation-only; it is not an implementation or repair dispatcher.** (13 Aug 2026, operator; clarifies D158) | Installed Codex 0.147.0 has no top-level custom-agent selector, so a standalone `codex exec` cannot truthfully claim the registered `engineer-be`, `engineer-fe`, or `repair` role boundary. The opt-in parent-only launcher may send a maintainer-selected prompt to Codex or Claude through fixed argv and stdin solely for bounded usage measurement or validation. Its role field is attribution metadata, not agent selection, and it must reject protected implementation-role labels. It never authors or repairs a PR slice, does not change routing, and cannot substitute for native named-agent dispatch under D158. Capturing native implementation-worker consumption remains a follow-up measurement problem rather than weakening the named-role topology or overstating provenance. |
| D160 | **A separate parent-only capture path may launch a native registered Claude implementation worker and record its metadata-only usage in the same process; this does not relax D159's measurement-only `run` command.** (14 Aug 2026, operator; roastpilot-agent#816) | The Codex parent may construct and execute fixed `claude --agent engineer-be` or `claude --agent engineer-fe` argv, send only the maintainer-ratified implementation contract and lead-authored directives on stdin, and parse the resulting bounded stream without persisting the prompt or raw output. Role attribution is truthful only because the recorder itself selects the registered project agent and owns that exact child process; detached ingestion, caller-supplied role envelopes, and Claude `repair` attribution are rejected. The registered agent remains the implementation authority boundary, while D158's capacity router remains the sole selector of family and role: capture is instrumentation, never routing authority. Persist only closed normalized metadata and usage totals, keep `whole_tree_verified=false` until a bounded parent-owned live proof establishes descendant coverage for the exact family/version, and run live proofs only on an idle non-roasting host. The existing `capture-agent-usage run` command remains measurement/validation-only and continues rejecting protected roles. Codex-native worker capture remains deferred until a read-only live proof shows that the current parent can observe role-, parent-, model-, effort-, and token-bound native events; an experimental schema or synthetic fixture alone is not provenance. |
| D161 | **For future parent-owned native Claude implementation measurements, the exact project session transcript tree is the authoritative token source; local Claude transcript persistence is explicitly accepted for this path.** (15 Aug 2026, operator; supersedes D160's native-path no-persistence requirement and roastpilot-agent#816's transcript non-goal) | The parent supplies a fresh UUID with Claude's supported `--session-id`, selects only registered `engineer-be` or `engineer-fe`, and may omit `--no-session-persistence` so Claude writes its documented plaintext `~/.claude/projects/<project>/<session>.jsonl` plus `<session>/subagents/*.jsonl` tree. This is an explicit privacy trade: the Claude-owned transcript may contain prompts, responses, tool output, and file contents under Claude's configured retention, but the RoastPilot usage sink and errors still contain closed normalized metadata only and never copy transcript content or host paths. After the owned process exits, the parent securely resolves only the expected project/session tree, bounds every read, rejects symlinks/non-regular files/schema or version drift, and sums usage once per `(session_id, parent-or-agent_id, message.id)`; byte-identical duplicate usage is counted once and conflicting duplicates fail closed. `whole_tree_verified=true` is permitted only when the parent transcript and every referenced subagent transcript are complete, internally consistent, and joined to the selected role/model/effort; otherwise no complete native record is written. Existing transcripts without a pre-bound task/slice/session mapping are not policy evidence. D159's measurement-only `run`, D158 routing/role boundaries, and the Codex-native deferral remain unchanged. |
| D162 | **RP-A recovery v2 predicts a temperature miss at the joint-objective DTR upper edge, may spend three further DTR percentage points of bounded recovery runway, and then releases recovery authority unconditionally.** (15 Aug 2026, operator; roastpilot-agent#708, under D122/D124 and after D155-D157/#781) | The independent projection trigger evaluates the target drop temperature at `target_development_percent + 2` percentage points, preserving D124's DTR HIT window rather than redefining success: while the window still has positive runway, `bean_temp_c + smoothed_ror_c_per_min * minutes_to_that_deadline` more than `projection_margin_c = 3.0` °C below the target for the existing three computed confirmation ticks enters recovery. Entry applies the already-ratified, non-compounding `recovery_entry_step_pp = 10` boost to the same D96 hard ceiling `min(heat_ceiling_percent, heat_engage_percent + recovery_headroom_percentage_points)`; it never lowers heat and repeated recovery cycles gain no authority. Once projection-triggered recovery is active, continuation projects to `target_development_percent + 5` percentage points: reaching the target temperature in that projection for three computed ticks begins the existing bounded glide, while reaching the +5 cutoff releases recovery authority unconditionally even if temperature remains short. The +5 point is an experimental recovery cutoff, NOT a widened quality target, automatic-drop rule, or permission to alter the existing joint-objective drop policy. Missing, non-finite, non-positive-runway, clock-regressing, or otherwise invalid projection inputs make the projection path inert; the pre-existing RoR-error recovery path remains available and flag-off behaviour stays byte-identical. The 196 °C ceiling guard, every safety evaluation, restart non-resumption, fan-ceiling one-way latch, and all existing hard heat ceilings remain unchanged. | D174 | **Ambient-doctrine scoring evidence is a conservative per-run derivation over retained DEVELOPMENT telemetry snapshots plus ordered recovery-event state; it never treats charge-time ambient as doctrine use and never claims advisor-consult coverage.** (25 Aug 2026, operator-ratified contract for roastpilot-agent#742; #811 pilot sample 10) | The trustworthy positive claim is only that usable fresh ambient input was durably observed while the ambient doctrine was effective. The numerator is retained DEVELOPMENT snapshots proven fresh after a finite `ambient_status.last_reading_monotonic_seconds` token changes within one uninterrupted tick episode; the denominator is all retained DEVELOPMENT snapshots while the run remains provably doctrine-effective. The MCP stamp is an opaque identity token: compare it only for equality, never subtract it, never compare it across a `tick` reset, and reset corroboration on a reset or unusable row. After corroboration, age is conservatively derived only from timezone-aware retained-snapshot UTC chronology and the frozen `max_reading_age_seconds`; missing, malformed, non-finite, stopped, unavailable, backwards-within-episode, zero/one-snapshot, or uncorroborated evidence fails to `not_proven`. A populated charge-time `ambient_temp_c` and the sampled fraction are explicitly not proof that the advisor reasoned on ambient, nor exact 1 Hz controller-tick coverage. Recovery keeps frozen `roast_runs.config_json` byte-immutable and enriches only the persisted copy of each existing `RECOVERY_REQUIRED` payload with the closed `ambient_fan_doctrine_recovery` grammar (`configured_enabled: bool`, `effective_enabled: bool`, `state: preserved|retired`); the already-published SSE payload is unchanged, no new event kind or schema ships, and the diagnostic retirement warning gains the run id but is never authoritative. All recovery events remain ordered evidence: any legacy/malformed/missing payload is `unknown`, any retirement makes `ever_retired=true` and prevents positive proof even if a later recovery preserves or re-enables the doctrine, and a legacy run with no recovery may still be derived from its existing snapshots. A stable read-only store summary feeds RP-D corpus JSON and human output; RP-D HIT/scalar arithmetic is unchanged. No REST/SPA surface, controller-tick instrumentation, controller snapshot, actuation, safety verdict, provider path, hardware operation, or production artifact is authorised. | This resolves #742's two recorded silent-void siblings with their existing durable sources rather than inventing a second runtime sampling system: raw snapshots can prove conservative input availability, while only the run-bound recovery timeline can prove that a live doctrine was retired. Failing unknowns toward `not_proven` preserves scoring integrity; keeping the evidence label narrower than advisor use prevents a plausible charge-time or sampled-data value from being promoted into a claim the run cannot support. |
| D175 | **RP-D abnormal termination is classified relative to the first successfully executed bean drop; a fault or emergency stop proven strictly after that clean drop is retained as provenance but does not zero an otherwise valid score.** (26 Aug 2026, operator-ratified issue contract; roastpilot-agent#726 item 2; narrows D124 only) | D124's fail-closed penalty still applies to an abnormal termination before or at drop, including an emergency-stop safety verdict at or before the drop and a ceiling-guard drop attempt before the successful drop. Ceiling-guard evidence includes both a successful ceiling-guard drop and a failed `drop_beans` attempt carrying the typed ceiling-guard reason, so a transient guard failure followed by a successful drop under another typed reason remains abnormal; classification never relies only on the successful event's reason text. The first successfully executed `drop_beans` event is the boundary. Evidence within `roast_events` is positioned by durable insertion `id`, not resettable tick chronology. Evidence from another persisted table is compared only through parseable UTC timestamps: strictly later is post-drop, while equality, malformed or missing timestamps, an unknown typed value, or an outcome that cannot be corroborated as post-drop fails closed to abnormal-before-or-at-drop. A same-tick first-crack plus ceiling-guard drop remains classifiable from command evidence even when normal telemetry throttling suppresses the boundary snapshot. A post-drop abnormal state is scorable only when the durable evidence proves the clean drop occurred first; it remains visibly distinguishable from a wholly normal run in stable machine-readable provenance. The shared classification is a read-only `RoastStore` API consumed by the RP-D scorer; the script-local SQL copy is retired. No controller persistence, command, transition, safety verdict, schema, telemetry throttle, advisor, MCP, REST, SPA, hardware operation, or production artifact changes are authorised. Typed plain enums remain the vocabulary and core logic never string-compares verdicts, phases, commands, event kinds, or drop reasons. | **Why:** the hardware pair recorded an accidental emergency stop during the cooling tail 2 minutes 43 seconds after a complete advisor drop. Treating every `faulted` outcome or every emergency-stop verdict anywhere in the run as a failed roast confuses post-drop cooling provenance with the roast's achieved outcome. The opposite shortcut is also unsafe for evidence quality: matching only an executed event's reason misses the ratified transient-failure trap, where the ceiling guard fired and failed before a later development-target drop succeeded. Anchoring one conservative classifier on the durable drop boundary preserves D124's Goodhart-resistant fail-closed rule wherever order is absent or ambiguous, while permitting only positively proven post-drop abnormalities to remain scorable. This decision changes historical evaluation classification only; it neither weakens live safety authority nor makes any hardware claim. |
| D176 | **RP-C's offline acceptance is decision-level, while RP-D outcome improvement remains a hardware-or-validated-simulator claim; the default-off joint-window planner uses a 3.0 °C temperature margin and a 30 s closing horizon.** (26 Aug 2026, operator; roastpilot-agent#710; narrows D122/D125) | A recorded replay can assess a candidate drop only at or before the historical drop. After that boundary the retained rows are cooling telemetry, not the counterfactual continued roast demanded by RP-C's ratified temperature-first policy, so no offline adapter may invent a later physical trajectory or claim an improved RP-D outcome. RP-C's offline acceptance is therefore: on the recorded temp-short fixtures the deterministic planner reports `TEMP_SHORT` before the DTR window closes, and the isolated c11-versus-c12 decision comparison withholds `should_drop` at the historical temperature-short drop points. RP-D joint-score improvement is reserved for #707's supervised single-variable hardware validation roast, or a validated #580 plant simulator. The planner is pure, stateless, advisory-context-only and default OFF; c12 is selectable only and c3 remains the live default. Its DTR window is the same target-plus-or-minus `drop_dev_margin_percent` already told to the advisor, not recovery-v2's separate +2/+5 horizons. The status temperature margin is **3.0 °C**, aligned to the ratified RP-D temperature tolerance, and `CLOSING` begins when the upper DTR boundary is at most **30 s** away. `TEMP_SHORT` takes precedence over `CLOSING`, so a closing window with a material temperature miss continues toward temperature; `CLOSING` means temperature is not materially short and the viable DTR window is expiring. Exact equality at target-minus-margin is not `TEMP_SHORT`; exact equality at the closing horizon is `CLOSING`. Missing/non-finite projection inputs produce one atomic absent block; zero or negative RoR remains valid evidence of a temperature-short trajectory. The effective target is capped by the enabled ceiling guard and the bitter ceiling so advice never treats an unreachable target as licence to hold through the enforced ceiling. No new drop authority, command, safety verdict, transition, restart behaviour, telemetry schema, MCP surface, hardware operation or production artifact is authorised. Recorded-fixture validation remains operator-local and commits no telemetry, run id, bean identity, date or outcome data. | **Why:** D125 already distinguishes fixed-trace decision evaluation from physical-outcome evaluation, but #710's original wording still required an offline RP-D improvement precisely where its intended intervention moves the candidate drop beyond the recorded roast. That requirement is unmeasurable without inventing plant behaviour. Decision-level acceptance tests the mechanism the software actually controls, while the later hardware or validated-simulator gate tests the physical result honestly. The 3.0 °C margin uses the same outcome tolerance that defines a meaningful temperature miss; the 30 s horizon gives the normal five-second advisory cadence several opportunities to act without labelling most of DEVELOPMENT as closing. Both are config-owned experimental defaults, not prompt literals, and the whole surface stays dormant until explicitly selected. |
| D177 | **Enabling RP-C's joint-window planner requires the deterministic ceiling guard, and `AHEAD` means the effective target temperature is projected to be reached by the opening of the DTR window.** (27 Aug 2026, operator; roastpilot-agent#710; amends D176) | Configuration is invalid when `joint_window_planner.enabled=True` and `ceiling_guard_drop_enabled=False`; the dependency is enforced by typed configuration validation, not by prompt prose or a runtime string check. This preserves the hard upper boundary required by the ratified temperature-first instruction: c12 may advise accepting DTR overrun while temperature is materially short, but it never operates without the controller-owned ceiling guard that terminates the hold. The pure planner remains total over explicit inputs and retains D176's effective-target cap, but no supported live configuration may enable it without the guard. Status classification is one ordered, disjoint chain: `TEMP_SHORT` first, then `CLOSING`, then `AHEAD`, otherwise `ON_TRACK`. `AHEAD` applies when projected bean temperature at the DTR-window opening is greater than or exactly equal to the effective target; `ON_TRACK` is the remaining valid case, where the target is not projected to be met by opening and neither higher-priority status applies. The boundary equality is therefore `AHEAD`. No new drop authority, safety verdict, command, transition, restart behaviour or hardware claim is authorised. | **Why:** without the ceiling guard, the temperature-first c12 teaching could encourage continued roasting with only an advisory bitter-ceiling value and no deterministic controller-owned stop, contradicting the operator's requirement that over-development remain bounded. Making the dependency a configuration invariant fails closed before a roast. The `AHEAD` boundary completes D176's four-state vocabulary without adding another control action: reaching the temperature objective by window opening is genuinely ahead of the joint constraint, while later feasible progress remains on track. |
| D178 | **RP-C's stochastic c11-versus-c12 acceptance uses one pre-registered 32-result comparison with an explicit control-reproduction gate; the earlier indeterminate run remains evidence and may not be retried until favourable.** (28 Aug 2026, operator; roastpilot-agent#710; amends only D176's decision-level acceptance protocol) | The comparison uses the same two authorised private recorded temp-short fixtures, the same historical temperature-short drop point per fixture, and prompt version as the only experimental variable. It obtains eight independent scored decisions per prompt arm per fixture: 32 scored results total. **FAIL** if any c12 decision has `should_drop=true`, regardless of c11. **PASS** only when every c12 decision has `should_drop=false` and, on each fixture separately, at least five of eight c11 decisions have `should_drop=true`. **INDETERMINATE** when c12 has no drop leak but c11 fails that majority gate on either fixture, or when the complete fixed run cannot be obtained. Baseline variability therefore never conceals a c12 failure; it decides only whether clean c12 withholding can be attributed to the c12 teaching rather than to a control arm that did not reproduce the historical drop tendency. The verdict is final for this protocol and is not an invitation to rerun. Calls are fresh and conversation-independent, executed once in a pre-registered interleaved order with no early stopping or outcome peeking. Provider-internal transport retries are disabled. Up to eight additional requests may replace transport/no-response attempts only; any semantic or parseable response is never discarded or retried, and an invalid semantic result makes the run incomplete. The hard ceiling is 40 provider requests and the pre-registered estimated spend ceiling is USD 0.80 at the existing USD 0.02 per-request planning estimate; reaching either ceiling yields `INDETERMINATE`. The harness records exact request count, fixed model/config and prompt/fixture/harness hashes locally, but raw fixtures, rows, identifiers, prompts, responses, dates, temperatures, bean identity and outcome data remain private and uncommitted. Public reporting is restricted to `PASS`, `FAIL`, or `INDETERMINATE`; it includes no aggregate arm counts and makes no RP-D physical-outcome claim. The previously recorded `INDETERMINATE` remains in the issue history and is not overwritten. A PASS permits a final status-only implementation PR to update the authoritative registry and close #710 while c12 remains selectable and default-off. FAIL or INDETERMINATE leaves #710 open and c12 selectable/default-off; no further provider calls are authorised without another operator-ratified plan decision. No hardware operation, controller/safety/MCP/runtime behaviour, production artefact, #709 work, or new backlog issue is authorised. | **Why:** D176 fixed the decision-level question but did not define how stochastic advisor output should be sampled or how a variable c11 control affects attribution. The first complete provider-backed attempt therefore produced an indeterminate result under an unratified unanimity rule. Repeating that harness until it returns a favourable result would select on outcome. This one-shot protocol separates the safety-relevant treatment failure rule from the control-reproduction gate, bounds spend and retries before observation, and remains deliberately descriptive: two fixtures cannot support a population-level significance or physical-roast claim. |
| D179 | **Visual-regression tolerance is scoped to the source of nondeterminism: only screenshots containing the unmasked uPlot canvas may use the D26 loose budget, while other screenshots use a substantially tighter per-spec budget and materially new UI regions receive locator-scoped visual contracts.** (28 Aug 2026, operator; roastpilot-agent#653; narrows D26) | The suite-wide `maxDiffPixelRatio: 0.01` default is retired because a page-level one-percent allowance can hide the addition or removal of a modest low-contrast control region. The D26 unmasked-canvas decision remains unchanged: page screenshots that actually contain the uPlot canvas keep an explicitly named, per-call loose allowance sufficient for the residual Skia/anti-aliasing variation observed in the pinned Linux Playwright image. Screenshots without that canvas use an explicitly named substantially tighter per-spec allowance; locator screenshots default to exact comparison unless the same pinned environment proves a smaller non-zero allowance is necessary. Exact numeric values are derived empirically only by repeated comparison in the repository's pinned `linux/amd64` Playwright container and are then encoded as shared typed test constants, never guessed from macOS output. Any materially new UI region added to an existing page-level baseline must also have a stable locator-scoped screenshot so its presence, dimensions, and pixels cannot be diluted by the full-page ratio. Documentation and a static contract test must make the three classes and the opt-in nature of the loose canvas allowance reviewable; weakening a non-canvas or locator class back to the loose allowance must fail that guard. Baselines remain generated and diffed only in the pinned producer image; the complementary chart-data assertions, fixed viewport, `deviceScaleFactor: 1`, font/animation settle protocol, replay fixtures, and D26's prohibition on masking the chart remain unchanged. | **Why:** roastpilot-agent#637 demonstrated that a visibly new dark-theme panel produced about 19,281 perceptually differing pixels yet remained within the global one-percent page budget. The page-local locator snapshot closed that instance, but the shared default still allowed the same false negative elsewhere. The residual instability that justified D26's budget belongs to the unmasked canvas, not to every DOM-only page and element screenshot. Scoping the allowance preserves chart visual-smoke coverage while restoring sensitivity for stable DOM chrome, and the locator rule makes a new region's disappearance fail independently of its fraction of the page. This is test-infrastructure policy only: it changes no SPA runtime state, API, controller, safety verdict, provider path, roast evidence, hardware operation, or production artefact, and it authorises no new backlog issue. |
| D180 | **The docs-only CI fast path (#702) activates via a gate/worker job split with base-trusted execution of both the classifier and the aggregate helper; the workflow-file residual is out of base-trusting's reach and stays contained by D118.** (29 Aug 2026, implementation worker on ratified `story-planner` contract; roastpilot-agent#702 slice 3; supersedes no prior decision, builds on the slice-1 classifier and slice-2a gate helper committed inertly under D169/D179-adjacent infrastructure work) | `ci.yml`'s three required checks (`Checks`, `Web (lint + typecheck + unit)`, `Web (Playwright snapshots)`) become tiny always-on gate jobs — one trusted-base checkout plus one `scripts/ci_gate_result.py` invocation — fed by `*-worker` jobs that run only when `classify.outputs.mode != 'docs-only'`, plus a `docs-fastpath` job that runs only when it is: whitespace/diff validation, a new minimal `docs-ci` dependency group (a subset of `dev` at identical pins, excluding `coffee-roaster-mcp` and its ML/audio stack), the `(docs or docs_ci) and not stress` pytest lane, and a real, authenticated Codecov upload (never a synthetic status). Both `scripts/ci_gate_result.py` (frozen interface, no behavioural edit) and `scripts/ci_change_classifier.py` now execute from a `.ci-gate-base` checkout resolved to the pull request's base SHA (or the default branch on `push`/`workflow_dispatch`) rather than the pull request's own copy — base-trusted execution — with the `classify` job's step order fixed at head-checkout, base-checkout, run (order is load-bearing: a base checkout before the head checkout would be deleted by it). The classifier gains a bounded per-Git-call timeout (20 s) and a total worktime budget (60 s, checked before each subsequent call so an exceeded budget issues no further call); this hardening is inert on its own introducing PR (which runs the base copy from `main`) and takes effect starting with the next PR — a residual explicitly named, not hidden. `codeql.yml` gains a matching `classify` job (SHA-pinned checkout matching this file's existing convention, narrowed to `contents: read` so it does not inherit the workflow's `security-events: write`) and `analyze` now needs it under the same docs-only skip condition; every non-`pull_request` trigger is unaffected because the classifier returns `full` for any non-`pull_request` event. The three prior slice-1/2a inertness governance tests ("no job may consume this yet") are replaced by equally mechanical consumption guards, not deleted. The five module-level `pytestmark = pytest.mark.docs` markers are replaced by exact per-function `@pytest.mark.docs` decorators on only the functions that actually read committed `docs/**/*.md` content, via an AST reader-detector extended for non-literal path construction (`.joinpath`, `os.path.join`, f-strings, `str.format`, loop-bound glob aliases, and a bounded same-module helper/fixture call graph) that fails the governance test closed, with a named file:line reason, on an unresolved dynamic read receiver rather than silently under- or over-marking; a new `docs_ci` marker covers focused fast-path tooling/workflow-structure tests that do not themselves read committed Markdown. **Named residual (not closed by this decision):** base-trusted execution removes the classifier/gate-helper *bytes* from pull-request control but cannot remove the *workflow file itself* from `pull_request`-event control, since GitHub always runs the head workflow definition for that event; containment remains the existing D118 privileged-review-override path plus these `main`-side governance tests, exactly as already documented in `AGENTS.md`. **Evidence posture — observed only, nothing claimed beyond it:** this row is written from the implemented, committed diff on branch `feature/702-docs-only-ci-fast-path-3` (base `fa221a1`). Every exact `ci.yml` lane command (quality, ordinary/serial/stress×3/package pytest, coverage combine+normalize, and the web lint/typecheck/build/coverage suite) ran locally against a fresh worktree-local venv and passed, and the `docs-fastpath` pytest command additionally ran end to end in a freshly provisioned `docs-ci`-only venv (70/6395 tests selected, coverage.xml normalized cleanly), empirically confirming the minimal dependency-group tier. **Corrected 29 Aug 2026 (second lead audit pass, same delegation, same commit `382b089`, no source fix needed):** the exact, literal ratified section 3.9 M1-M28 mutation table (not a reconstruction) was executed — the full per-item mutation/guard-test/result record lives in `roastpilot-agent`'s `docs/state/registry.md` (the per-slice evidence log; not duplicated verbatim here to keep this row a stable summary). Each of the 28 items was applied to the real committed file (never via git — snapshot/restore by `cp`, byte-identity confirmed via `diff` after every restore), proven to fail its named guard test red with real pytest output, then restored and reconfirmed green, including M22 (the rename/copy both-path allowlist check replaced with destination-only admission), whose named guard test `test_ci_change_classifier.py::test_boundary_crossing_or_unusual_path_is_full` genuinely failed on exactly its `(C100, src/old.py, docs/new.md)` case (source outside the docs grammar, destination inside it) while the sibling `R100` case correctly stayed green under the identical mutation. Coverage: `scripts/ci_change_classifier.py` still measures 100% statement and 100% branch coverage (126 stmts / 50 branches, 0 missed). `cd web && npx playwright test` was run for real **inside the exact SHA-pinned Linux/amd64 container the `web-snapshots-worker` job uses** (`ghcr.io/syamaner/roastpilot-agent/playwright:v1.55.1-noble`, pulled via `docker pull --platform linux/amd64`), with the current worktree mounted and no baseline regeneration: 36 of 37 passed; the one failure (`dashboard.spec.ts` `dashboard-fault`, an off-by-one chart-scale bound) passed clean on an isolated re-run inside the same container, and this branch's diff touches zero files under `web/` (`git diff --stat` against `origin/main`'s merge-base is empty there), so it is a confirmed pre-existing flake outside this slice's scope, not a regression. All host-generated container artefacts (`web/test-results/`, the two isolated Docker volumes for `node_modules`/`dist`) were removed after the run. A full single-process pytest gate with `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` stripped (no `ROASTPILOT_*` vars were set to begin with) passed clean: EXIT=0, 6395 tests collected, no failures; `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` (220 files), and `pyright` strict all passed clean. No proof pull request was opened and no *hosted* GitHub Actions/CodeQL run was observed under this delegation (branches committed, not pushed, per explicit operator instruction), so the disposable docs-only/mixed proof-PR pair, the `main`-push full-coverage Codecov observation, the live CodeQL docs-only skip, and the D118 merge-lifecycle dispatch remain **pending** and are not claimed here — but the GHCR-container Playwright snapshot run itself is no longer pending: it was executed, locally, inside the authoritative pinned image. #702 stays open pending the remaining hosted-only evidence. | **Why:** the prior two slices (#863, #871) deliberately committed the classifier and the aggregate gate helper inertly, each proven safe in isolation before either could decide a real outcome; this slice is the single remaining activation step the story-planner contract scoped, and it had to solve the "decided by pull-request-controlled bytes" defect class for both the classifier and the helper at once, or the second one would have reopened the same hole the first one closed. Stating the residual and the pending evidence explicitly, rather than letting a green implementation diff imply a proven activation, keeps this repository's registry and this table from drifting the way D23/D108-D118's PR-review history warns against. |

| D181 | **The final #702 activation keeps D180's fail-closed docs-only fast path but binds every trusted checkout and aggregate input to an exact identity, scopes marker governance to the current repository plus explicitly supported static patterns, and does not claim to interpret arbitrary future Python.** (1 Sep 2026, operator amendment and completed activation evidence; roastpilot-agent#702, PR #872; narrows and corrects D180's final implementation/evidence clauses) | On `pull_request`, trusted classifier and aggregate-helper bytes remain pinned to `pull_request.base.sha`; on every non-PR trusted checkout they use the event's exact `github.sha`, never a moving default-branch tip. A new unsupported test shape first arrives through a non-doc change and therefore receives full CI and review; the five known unsupported hypothetical forms (`__len__`, a bare `parametrize` alias with docs values, `functools.partial` reader callbacks, implicit `__getitem__`, and `os.chdir`/`contextlib.chdir` followed by a relative read) are recorded in roastpilot-agent#932 rather than misrepresented as fixed. The finite marker boundary is the only honest static-analysis contract: repeatedly adding synthetic Python execution forms without a current omitted test had become an unbounded attempt to model the language, while silently calling known gaps fixed would corrupt the governance record. Recording the supported boundary and follow-up preserves fail-closed review of future test architecture without delaying a fully evidenced activation. No controller, safety verdict, command path, recovery behaviour, provider call, SPA runtime, roast data, hardware operation, or production authority changes. |

| D182 | **The torch-free inference frontend used by `coffee-roaster-mcp` is MCP-owned as a narrow NumPy/SciPy runtime component, with a cross-repository parity contract; MCP does not depend on the current full `coffee-first-crack-detection` package.** (1 Sep 2026, operator; coffee-roaster-mcp#157 planning; narrows D27 Phase 2 and corrects its obsolete library boundary) | Phase 1 remains model-repository authority at `coffee-first-crack-detection` commit `749c9330b01c93fa153cca2e290ebfbd6c1d986c`, where `MelFrontend` reproduces the trained AST/Kaldi preprocessing path without Torch or Transformers. MCP owns its runtime integration and defensive parsing of `preprocessor_config.json`; committed golden fixtures and numerical parity tests bind the port to the Phase 1 reference. The packaged acceptance boundary is a built MCP wheel that imports and performs real ONNX-int8 inference in a clean environment with neither Torch nor Transformers installed. The current full model package is not an allowed MCP runtime dependency because its base dependency surface still includes the training stack. Creating a future shared inference-only package remains a separate decision and must not delay #157 by implication. This decision selects ownership and dependency boundaries only: it does not ratify a story-planner contract, PR plan, release version, validation threshold, hardware session or publication. | **Why:** depending on the full model package would reintroduce the heavyweight dependencies D27 is meant to remove, while first creating another published inference package adds an unnecessary release and sequencing boundary before #157. | D183 | **Pi 5 validation for coffee-roaster-mcp#157/#194 uses one primary audio stream with recording default-off, a two-phase characterisation, pre-ratified acceptance thresholds, and one all-or-nothing supervised evidence run; it does not validate multi-stream Pi recording.** (1 Sep 2026, operator; narrows D64 for the E11 Pi path and supplies D27 Phase 2's hardware-evidence contract) | The target is one primary microphone/capture stream. `recording.enabled` and `recording.autocapture` remain `false` by default and recording is configuration-only: validation explicitly sets both true, configures no additional recording device, and tees the detector stream to one WAV. Multi-stream Pi capture is outside #157/#194 and needs a later decision. The exact Pi 5 board/RAM, 64-bit OS/kernel/firmware, PSU/cooling, storage, Python, candidate MCP wheel SHA-256, dependency inventory, ONNX-int8/model/config hashes, ALSA primary-device identity and Hottop serial identity are recorded and frozen; any material identity change restarts characterisation. Characterisation is one supervised, no-actuation 60-minute session: 30 minutes of real ONNX inference with recording off, then 30 minutes with primary recording on against the warmer system, while Hottop serial telemetry remains read-only. Structured evidence adds per-input capture-run-lifetime `max_consecutive_overflow_count`, aggregates active inputs with `max()` rather than addition, resets it with `total_overflow_count`, preserves the frozen final snapshot, and treats any unexpected capture restart as a failed run. It also adds monotonic `last_inference_duration_ms`, `max_inference_duration_ms` and `inference_overrun_count`. After characterisation the operator ratifies a non-negative `N` below the unchanged fatal streak limit of 30, and a concrete `X` equal to the permitted peak trailing-60-second lost-audio milliseconds; both are locked before acceptance and cannot be loosened from that run's outcome. Acceptance additionally requires no fatal/mic error, zero dropped windows and overruns, maximum inference duration below the configured seven-second hop, no growing queue, a complete primary WAV/sidecar, and the initially approved provisional host limits: abort/alarm at 80 degrees Celsius, clean current and sticky under-voltage/capping/throttling bits from the validation boot, at least 512 MiB `MemAvailable`, at least 2 GiB free before start and 1 GiB throughout. Those resource limits may be revised before characterisation; a material later revision requires fresh qualifying evidence. Final acceptance is a separate supervised live session of at least 20 continuous minutes. The human operator alone owns physical connection, roast start, every Hottop action, abort and emergency response; agents and collectors send no hardware writes, and any collector alarm requires human action. One immutable run may evidence both issues only when both separate matrices pass; failure of either closes neither. Full private evidence stays outside Git, is SHA-256-manifested, and is copied after the session to `/Users/sertanyamaner/Documents/RoastPilot/validation-evidence/pi5/<run-id>/`; the Pi copy remains until the laptop copy verifies, neither copy is automatically deleted, and retention lasts at least through #157/#194 closure, the verified MCP minor release and roastpilot-agent#137 completion. Git may receive only a sanitised report and private-manifest hash. The intended release is the next minor, currently 0.2.0; the operator owns tag and publication, followed by a clean published-package Pi smoke. | **Why:** the actual E11 target cannot sustain the earlier two-stream plan, and recording must remain opt-in outside an explicitly observed validation session. Separating recording-off and recording-on characterisation attributes pressure to inference/capture versus the recorder instead of locking an uninterpretable threshold. Structured maxima and inference timing make the proposed gates measurable, pre-ratification prevents tuning acceptance to its own result, and all-or-nothing evidence prevents selective reuse. The live roast proves real coexistence only under direct human authority; no LLM controls hardware and no evidence collector becomes an actor. Recording this decision authorises no hardware operation, implementation, release or public claim. |

| D184 | **coffee-roaster-mcp adopts D158's Codex-led, contract-first principles through repository-specific contracts and a governance-first slice; RoastPilot Agent roles do not govern it unchanged, and GitHub-hosted Claude is not used.** (1 Sep 2026, operator; delivery topology for coffee-roaster-mcp#157/#194) | Human product and hardware authority is unchanged. Changes to `audio.py`, `first_crack_runtime.py` or `detector.py` trigger the MCP safety lens. Before #157 implementation, slice 0 lands the MCP topology/one-PR-per-slice rule, refreshes its stale registry and release authority, removes hosted-Claude workflows, defines the MCP gate set, and enables `main` protection requiring the current `Checks` and `Build Package` checks in strict/current mode with conversation resolution, admin enforcement and no force-push or deletion.
